**Decision, host names:** `fernjo_ssh::validate_host` (`known_hosts.rs`) is the single rule, and `host_port` (the one place that turns host and port into the `known_hosts` name) runs it, so `check`, `stored_algorithms`, `add` and `replace` all return `SshError::InvalidHost` (code `invalid_host`) for a host it refuses and never write anything. `Session::connect` calls it first, before any file access or network traffic, and `connect_plan::resolve` calls it so the user gets the existing `invalid_host` command error ("the host name contains characters that are not allowed (use a DNS name or an IP address)") before a session or tab exists. Accepted: 1 to 255 bytes; without a colon, only letters, digits, `.`, `-` and `_` (a trailing dot, `my_host`, `web-01.lab`, an IPv4 literal, punycode `xn--...`); with a colon, only an IPv6 literal that `std::net::Ipv6Addr` parses, optionally followed by `%zone` (non-empty, letters, digits, `.`, `-`, `_`). Everything else is refused, in particular whitespace and control characters (a newline starts a second line), `,` (alias), a leading `|`, `@`, `!` or `#` (hashed entry, marker, negation, comment), `*` and `?` (wildcards), quotes, brackets and `/`. Brackets are refused in the input because `host_port` generates the `[host]:port` form itself from host and port (a user-typed `[::1]` would not resolve anyway: the connect passes the host to the resolver as it is). **Non-ASCII UTF-8:** letters and digits (`char::is_alphanumeric`) are kept, so a name such as `bücher.example` still works: the connect hands the name to the system resolver unchanged, and mDNS or `/etc/hosts` names with such letters can resolve, so refusing them would reject legitimate input; the injection characters are all ASCII, and Unicode whitespace, control and format characters (U+0085, U+2028, U+200B) fall outside `is_alphanumeric` and are refused. The existing ASCII-only lowercasing and case-insensitive comparison stay the same on the write and the read side, so a name with upper-case non-ASCII letters still matches itself. Tests (`known_hosts.rs`): the writer refuses the review's probes (`evil,victim.corp`, a newline, a space, an empty host, a 256-byte host) and 30 more forms (35 hosts in all) on ports 22 and 2222 for all four operations and leaves the file byte for byte as it was, and a table of legitimate hosts (DNS names with `-`, `_`, trailing dot, punycode, IPv4, `::1`, `2001:db8::1`, `fe80::1%eth0`, `fe80::1%12`, an IPv4-mapped literal, two non-ASCII names, a 255-byte name) round-trips on both ports (`add`, then `check` is `Known` for exactly that host, also in upper case, `Unknown` for another host or the other port, then `replace`); `a_host_that_cannot_be_recorded_in_known_hosts_is_refused` in `connect_plan.rs` covers the plan level. An already stored connection with a host that is now refused (for example an imported `a,b`) fails to open with `invalid_host` and can be corrected in the properties panel.
**Rationale:** One allow-list at the single place that builds the file name cannot be bypassed by a new caller; checking again at the connect start and in the plan gives the user a clear error before anything happens, and an allow-list is simpler to review than a deny-list of metacharacters.
**Rejected alternatives:** validating only in `connect_plan::resolve` and the importer (the writer would still trust every future caller); a deny-list of the dangerous characters (misses the next one); escaping the host in the file (OpenSSH has no escape syntax for these); rejecting all non-ASCII names (would break names the resolver can resolve); reading only the 166-byte header in `open` and leaving the whole-file check to `unlock` (two states for "too big"; a vault that opens but can never unlock); a `VaultError::TooLarge` variant (the UI already treats `unreadable` as "unusable" and shows the reason, a new code buys nothing).

## 2026-11-18 – M6 final review fixes: vault write cap, same-parent reorder, theme mirror
**Context:** The final whole-branch review of M6 found three places where one half of the code accepted what the other half refuses.
**Decision:** **Vault:** `Vault::save` checks the file size (header + plaintext + tag, before encrypting) against `MAX_FILE_LEN` and refuses with `save_failed` ("the vault would be larger than 64 MiB, the most that can be opened again"); nothing is written, `vault.fernjo` and `.bak` stay as they were, memory is rolled back like for any save error. Before, a big enough vault was written and the next start made it `Unusable`. The error reuses `save_failed` (every caller and the UI already handle it) instead of a new variant, like the load side reuses `unreadable`. **Tree:** `Tree::move_node` skips the depth check when the new parent is the current parent (and inserts without it): a move inside one parent keeps every level, so siblings of a tree that an older version made deeper than `MAX_TREE_DEPTH` (it still loads up to about level 62) can be reordered; any move to another parent is still checked, so nothing can get deeper. **Theme mirror:** `ThemeStore` writes the `localStorage` copy of the theme only when Rust answers `persistent: true`; a run under `FERNJO_DATA_DIR` (System, `persistent: false`) still applies the attribute but no longer overwrites the real run's copy, which made the next real start flash once. The mirror is applied when `main.ts` runs, not before the first frame, and without it the system theme applies until the settings have loaded.
**Rejected alternatives:** a `VaultError::TooLarge` variant (a new code for a case no caller treats differently); a larger load cap (the cap protects every start from a planted file); skipping the depth check for every move that does not make the tree deeper than it already is (more code for a case nobody asked for).

## 2026-11-18 – M6 security fixes: device-local folder

`Location.device_dir` / `AppState.device_dir` name a folder for state that must not travel with the synced data folder (vault generation marks, hosts confirmed on this device). Normally `<OS local data dir>/fernjo-device` (`%LOCALAPPDATA%`, `~/.local/share`, `~/Library/Application Support`); it is flat and independent of the chosen data folder, because anything below the data folder would sync with it. With `FERNJO_DATA_DIR` set it is `<FERNJO_DATA_DIR>/.device`, so scratch runs never touch real device state (a synced folder handed in through the variable loses the protection). Without an OS local data dir it is `None` and the consumers stay in memory. The path is only computed (`device_dir_from`); the consumers create it. `apply_data_folder` refuses (`folder_device_dir`) a folder that is, contains or lies inside the device folder.

## 2026-11-18 – M6 security fixes: vault rollback protection

**Context:** a sync tool or another writer of the synced folder can put an older valid `vault.fernjo` back; nothing noticed (finding 4 of the v0.1.0 security review). Deleted entries or an old master password's version would silently return.
**Decision:** the encrypted body carries `generation: u64` (a missing field reads as 0, the header version stays 1, older readers ignore the field; the golden fixture `crates/fernjo-vault/tests/fixtures/v1-no-generation.fernjo`, written by the build before the change, proves a real v1 file opens and stays byte-identical until its first save). Every save writes `max(body generation, floor) + 1`, including a master password change and a restore. Each device keeps the highest generation seen per vault id in `<device dir>/vault-marks.json` (`GenerationStore`: `FileGenerationStore` with an in-process copy so a failed write still protects the run, `MemoryGenerationStore` without a device folder and in tests); marks only increase, the file is re-read before every write, and an unreadable, corrupt or newer-schema file counts as empty. `unlock` and `unlock_with_key` refuse a body below the floor (`max(handle floor, mark)`) as `rolled_back { found, seen }`, only after the password or key opened the body, so rollback state is never revealed to someone without them; an equal or higher generation opens silently and raises the mark. `unlock_accepting_older` (command `vault_unlock_older`) opens it anyway, does not lower the floor and re-stamps the file at once with a best-effort save (`floor + 1`; `.bak` then keeps the pre-accept file; a failed re-stamp keeps the unlock and the question returns on the next start). Auto-unlock never accepts: `AutoUnlock::RolledBack`, `autoUnlockIssue: "rolled_back"`, the keychain item is kept. `AppState.generations` is built from `device_dir` (memory when there is none) and attached to the vault before the startup auto-unlock and to a newly created vault.
**Rationale:** the counter inside the authenticated body cannot be forged or stripped without the data key, and the mark lives where the sync cannot reach it, so "older than this device saw" is decidable offline. Owner decisions: no format version bump (a bump would lock older builds out of a file only a counter was added to), and accepting an older file rewrites it at once so every device converges on a newer generation.
**Limits:** the first open on a new device (or after the device folder is lost) has nothing to compare with; `connections.json` and `known_hosts` are not covered; a device folder inside a synced tree loses the protection; a bad actor with the data key or the device folder is out of scope. The password and import dialogs outside the vault note show the `rolled_back` error text but have no "open the older vault" button: that choice is made in the vault note. Two more limits: a build from before the counter drops the `generation` field on its next save, so the file reads as generation 0 again and a device on the new build gets a harmless repeated `rolled_back` prompt (accepting is overwritten by the next old-build save; relevant only for mixed-version use of several devices); and two devices that each save once from the same base both write generation N, the check is `<`, so the file the sync tool leaves is accepted silently and the other device's changes survive only in `.bak`.
**Rejected alternatives:** a timestamp or file mtime (sync tools rewrite it); a hash chain of versions (needs every version); storing the counter in the header (a header byte change would need a format bump); refusing silently or ignoring the rollback (the former behaviour).

## 2026-11-18 – M6 security fixes: hosts confirmed on this device

**Context:** anyone who can write to the synced data folder could repoint a connection and add a matching line to `known_hosts`; the next connect then showed no prompt and the handshake went on to authentication with the vault credential (finding of the v0.1.0 security review).
**Decision:** a key found in the synced `known_hosts` is accepted silently only when this device confirmed the same key for that host and port. The second layer is an OpenSSH-format file `<device_dir>/known_hosts.confirmed`, handled by the existing `KnownHosts` type (same parser, same `host_port` normalisation and host validation, same atomic and owner-only writes). `ConnectParams.confirmed_hosts: Option<PathBuf>` names it (`None` and no in-memory store: layer off, used by embedders and the SSH integration tests); `ConnectParams.confirmed_in_memory: Option<KnownHosts>` is `KnownHosts::in_memory()` (same matching and replace rules, the bytes live in a shared buffer) and is what the shell passes when `AppState.device_dir` is `None`, so a host asks once per run and a connect never fails for lack of a folder. The check stays inside the russh handshake (`handler.rs`), before authentication. `Interaction::host_key_synced` has a default that delegates to `host_key`, so existing implementers compile; `TauriInteraction` overrides it with `PromptView::HostKeySynced { host, port, keyType, fingerprint, previous }` and reuses `PromptAnswer::HostKey`. (Since M11 the variant has no `previous` field any more; see "M11 SSH: the device's confirmed keys outrank the synced `known_hosts`".)
Handler table (`decide`): known and confirmed `Known`: connect. Known and confirmed `Unknown` or an unreadable confirmed file: synced prompt; "Confirm and connect" adds, "Connect once" records nothing, "Cancel" is `host_key_rejected`. Known and confirmed `Changed{stored}`: the same prompt with `previous = stored`, and "Confirm and connect" replaces. Unknown: the old prompt; trust always writes `known_hosts` and then the confirmed file. Changed: the old prompt; replace writes `known_hosts` and then replaces in the confirmed file. A failed write of the confirmed file is ignored (worst case one more prompt); a failed write of `known_hosts` keeps the old rejection and nothing is confirmed. (Changed in M11, review #24: when the confirmed file holds any key for the host and the presented key is not among them, the changed-key prompt is shown instead, whatever `known_hosts` says, and `previous` is gone; see "M11 SSH: the device's confirmed keys outrank the synced `known_hosts`".)
**Owner decisions:** no seeding. Entries already in `known_hosts` are not copied into the confirmed file after the update, because that would also confirm whatever a writer already planted; every host asks once per device. The memory layer is used only when there is no device folder at all (`device_dir` is `None`; `SessionManager::host_files`): the host then asks once per run. When `device_dir` is set but unwritable, the confirmed file is still the layer, `confirm` fails silently (best effort) and the host asks on every connect.
**Why a device-local file:** the same reason as the vault generation marks: the sync cannot reach it, so a planted line in the synced folder cannot satisfy it. The location is `device_dir` (see "device-local folder").
**Rejected alternatives:** a MAC over `known_hosts` with a vault-derived key (the vault can be locked at connect time, a password change or a second device would need key distribution, and a writer who replays an older valid file still passes); binding trust to the credential id (it protects the credential but not the "first use of this host" decision, and multiplies prompts per credential); seeding the confirmed file from `known_hosts` (see above); failing the connect when the confirmed file cannot be written (a read-only profile would lock the user out).
**Limits:** a device folder inside a synced tree loses the protection; repointing a connection to a host this device already confirmed is not covered; the first prompt on a new device relies on the user's judgement. User-visible: every host asks once per device after the update.

## 2026-11-19 – M7: connections.json fingerprint check

**Context:** the data folder is synced between devices. `Store` held the tree in memory and saved it whole, so a `connections.json` that another device or a sync tool had replaced meanwhile was silently overwritten by the next edit (the `.bak` kept only the one version before).
**Decision:** `Store` keeps a SHA-256 `Fingerprint` of the bytes it last read or wrote (`persist::fingerprint`, read through `read_capped`; `persist::save` returns the hash of exactly what it wrote, trailing newline included; after the restore-from-`.bak` re-save in `open` it is the hash `save` returned, or the file's current hash when that save failed). `mutate`, `replace_tree` and `clear_credential` (every path that saves) call `check_unchanged` first: a missing file is recreated, identical bytes pass, any other difference is `StoreError::ChangedOnDisk` (code `changed_on_disk`) and nothing is written, no `.bak` either. `Store::reload` re-runs `persist::load`, replaces the tree and resets the fingerprint; on an error nothing changes. The UI offers "Reload" (a later task); the unsaved edit is dropped and nothing from the other device is lost.
**Rejected alternatives:** length plus mtime (sync tools can preserve both); keeping the pending edit for replay (about twice the work); a silent merge (unsafe for deletes and moves).
**Limits:** it is a check, not a lock: a sync tool can still win the race between the check and the rename. The writer behind `connections.json` (and `settings.json`, same `persist::write_atomic`) is not the hardened one of the vault and `known_hosts`: it still writes through the fixed `<file>.tmp` name (opened with create and truncate, so a symlink planted there is followed) and its `.bak` copy follows a planted symlink too. This needs write access to the data folder and is in the backlog ("Atomic writers: `.bak` symlink and `connections.json` temp name"). `sha2` 0.11.1 was already in `Cargo.lock` (via another crate), so no new package version.

## 2026-11-19 – M7: release binaries carry no build paths

**Context:** Rust embeds source paths in panic messages and debug info, so a CI-built binary named `/home/runner/...`, `C:\Users\runneradmin\.cargo\registry\...` and similar, which leaks the build environment and adds noise.
**Decision:** the release build passes `--remap-path-prefix` for `$GITHUB_WORKSPACE` (to `/build`) and the cargo home (to `/cargo`) through `RUSTFLAGS` (existing flags kept; on Windows both paths go through `cygpath -w` because rustc matches the native form). A step after the build runs `tools/check-no-build-paths.sh` on `target/[<triple>/]release/fernjo[.exe]`, a plain `grep -a -c` for the registry paths and the runner home names; exit 1 fails the job.
**Why a grep check:** the remap can silently miss a path form (Windows separators, `CARGO_HOME` outside `$HOME`); the byte search proves the result instead of the intent.
**Limits:** the check proves only the binary CI built, and only for the listed patterns; the script itself is tested on synthetic files. The proof on a real binary is the release dry run on the pull request.


## 2026-11-19 – M7: vault Low findings triage

**Context:** the M6 vault review left eight Low findings (roadmap block "Vault Low findings"). Each got a disposition; one proposal was flipped after the compiler showed a use.
**Decisions, final disposition of all eight rows:**
1. *KDF cost accepted at load (1 GiB, 20 passes, 16 lanes):* **fixed.** `KdfParams::validate` and `Header::parse` now cap each field at 4 times the shipped default (512 MiB, 24 passes, 16 lanes). A header above a cap is `unreadable` with a message naming the field and the limit (checked before `validate`, so it wins over `invalid_kdf_params`); the vault is `Unusable` and the file untouched. Backups and `create_with` share the bounds, so the app never writes what it refuses to read. `KdfParams::TEST` and the golden v1 fixture stay inside. The cap is per field, so a header exactly at all three caps is accepted: the Argon2 cost read from a vault header is bounded to at most four times the default per setting (about 16 times the default's work, because time grows with memory times passes; roughly 40% below the old 1 GiB x 20 passes), and anything above is refused as unreadable. Tests: `load_refuses_a_header_with_an_excessive_kdf_cost`, `load_accepts_exactly_four_times_the_default_cost`.
2. *Fixed temp name, symlinks, no `create_new`, no directory fsync:* **fixed.** `write_atomic` creates `<file>.<random>.tmp` with `create_new` (mode 0600 on Unix, up to 8 name retries) and, on Unix, `sync_all`s the folder after the rename (a failure of that sync does not fail the save, the file is already in place). The `.bak` copy and the rename are unchanged. `generation.rs` uses the same function. Tests: `save_never_writes_through_the_old_fixed_temp_name` (all platforms), `save_never_follows_a_planted_temp_symlink` (`#[cfg(unix)]`, compiled everywhere, run on Unix only), `write_atomic_uses_a_fresh_temp_name_and_leaves_none_behind`. `a_failed_restamp_...` blocked the old fixed name with a directory to force a failure; it now blocks `.bak` instead.
3. *Windows keychain item with Enterprise persistence:* **fixed.** `keyring` 4.2's own `Entry` has no modifiers, but its `Entry::inner` is a `keyring-core` entry and `windows-native-keyring-store` 1.1 accepts the `persistence` modifier. `OsKeyStore` now builds `keyring_core::Entry::new_with_modifiers(.., {"persistence": "Local"})` on Windows after `keyring::Entry::store_status()` has installed the store (new direct dependency `keyring-core` 1, the version already in `Cargo.lock`). The store rewrites the credential with the entry's persistence on every `set_secret`, so an existing Enterprise item becomes Local the next time the key is stored (the modifier is applied only when the secret is written: untick and tick "Unlock automatically on this device" in the vault manager to switch an existing item; a newly written item is Local at once). Proof: `os_keystore_round_trip` (ignored, real keychain) reads the `persistence` attribute back as `Local`.
4. *Secret residue in freed buffers:* **accepted.** Cleaning some copies cannot be proven complete (allocator, the argon2 matrix, serde and the OS), and an unlocked vault holds the secrets in memory anyway; the zeroising types (`Zeroizing`, `SecretString`, `DataKey`) stay where they are.
5. *`change_password` never upgrades the KDF cost:* **fixed.** The new header takes the field-wise maximum of the stored cost and `Vault.upgrade_target` (the default for an opened vault, the creation cost for one made with `create_with`, so tests stay fast), is validated, and the new password is derived at that cost. A stronger stored field is never lowered. Test: `change_password_upgrades_a_weak_cost_and_never_lowers_one`.
6. *Header re-encoded from parsed fields:* **accepted.** It only matters once `CURRENT_VERSION` is 2 (a v2 header must not be rewritten by v1 code, and v1 code refuses a newer version anyway); the golden test `golden_v1` fails first when the layout changes.
7. *Public `Secret` derives `Serialize`:* **accepted (proposal flipped).** Removing the derive does not compile: `fernjo-import`'s backup body (`backup.rs`, `BodyEntry`) serialises `Secret` into the sealed `FJBK` container, and `Entry` does inside the encrypted vault body. Both are encrypted-at-rest paths; the webview never receives a `Secret` (`reveal` is the one explicit exception, and `no_response_contains_a_secret` guards the commands). A narrower serialisation would need a second mirror type for no gain.
8. *`connections.json` / `settings.json` without size cap:* **done in Task 1** (`read_capped` on both); the vault file already uses `read_capped` with `MAX_FILE_LEN`.
**Limits:** the Unix-only symlink test and the directory fsync could not run on the Windows development machine; they compile everywhere and run in CI on Linux. Row 2 closes the temp-file symlink only: the temp name is random and created with `create_new`, but the `.bak` step (`fs::copy(path, &bak)` and `owner_only(&bak)` in `write_atomic`) still follows a symlink planted at `vault.fernjo.bak`, so "followed through symlinks" is fixed for the temp file and still open for the `.bak` copy. Exploiting it needs write access to the data folder (a peer or a sync tool that syncs symlinks); it is not a regression and is in the backlog ("Atomic writers: `.bak` symlink and `connections.json` temp name").

## 2026-11-19 – M7: SSH Low findings triage

Source: roadmap blocks "SSH Low findings" and "M6 security fixes: deferred follow-ups" (items 1, 4, 5). All nine rows, with the disposition and the reason:

1. *`replace` removes only the first matching entry of a hand-edited `h,H` line:* **fixed.** `parse_line` now collects every matching entry of the host field (`Match.entries`) and `rewrite_without` removes all of them. A line that still names other hosts keeps them (and their trust, key unchanged); a line left with no host is dropped. So no stale key stays for the replaced host and no other host loses trust. Test `replace_removes_every_matching_entry_of_a_comma_list_line`.
2. *`Unknown` plus "trust always" does not replace a key of the same type in the confirmed-hosts file (follow-up 5):* **fixed.** The handler's `confirm` no longer picks `add` or `replace` from the check result; it always calls `KnownHosts::trust` (= `replace`, which also works on an empty file), so a failed or stale check cannot leave an older key of the same type next to the new one. Test `trust_always_replaces_a_different_key_of_the_same_type`.
3. *Fixed temp name, no `create_new`, no lock (follow-up 1):* **fixed.** `known_hosts` and the confirmed file share `write_atomic`: random temp name with `create_new` and mode 0600 on Unix, `.bak` copy, rename, directory fsync on Unix (best effort), the same approach as the vault writer. `fernjo-ssh` does not depend on `fernjo-vault` and no shared crate exists, so the small helper is repeated (with `getrandom` as a direct dependency) instead of adding a crate for 40 lines. Every read-modify-write (`add`, `replace`, `trust`) runs under one process-wide `Mutex` (`WRITE_LOCK`), so two connects cannot lose each other's update. Another process (OpenSSH, a sync tool) is not covered by the lock. Tests `concurrent_confirmations_do_not_lose_an_update`, `write_never_uses_the_old_fixed_temp_name`, Unix-only `temp_file_is_never_a_fixed_name`.
4. *Server keyboard-interactive text and prompt count unbounded in the UI:* **fixed** in `sessions.rs` (`cap_ki_round`): at most 16 prompts, 1024 characters per prompt and for the round name, 4096 for the instructions, cut text gets an ellipsis. Only the view is capped; the answer must still match the server's original prompt count, so a capped round fails the login instead of answering prompts the user never saw. Test `ki_prompts_are_capped_in_count_and_length`.
5. *Key of another algorithm for a stored host reads as a new host; a key-type downgrade shows no `previous` (follow-up 4):* **accepted.** It asks and never trusts silently; the prompt shows the fingerprint of the presented key.
6. *`@revoked` ignored:* **accepted**, documented since M4 (marker lines never match).
7. *Secrets copied into plain `String`s for the wire:* **accepted.** The strings live only for the call; same reason as the vault residue.
8. *No host key re-check on re-key (russh):* **accepted.** Upstream behaviour.
9. *Windows agent pipe squatting:* **accepted.** Same trust model as OpenSSH for Windows.

**Limits:** the Unix-only symlink test could not run on the Windows development machine (it compiles everywhere and runs in CI on Linux); the SSH container tests were not run locally (CI runs them). Row 3 closes the temp-file symlink only: the `.bak` copy in `write_atomic` (`fs::copy(path, &bak)` and `owner_only(&bak)`) still follows a symlink planted at `known_hosts.bak` or `known_hosts.confirmed.bak`; it needs write access to the folder, is not a regression and is in the backlog ("Atomic writers: `.bak` symlink and `connections.json` temp name").

## 2026-11-19 – M7: import Low findings and M6 follow-ups triage

Source: roadmap blocks "Import and backup Low findings" and "M6 security fixes: deferred follow-ups" (items 2, 3). All eight rows, with the disposition and the reason:

1. *Unbounded attacker strings in error messages and in backup `createdAt` and `app`:* **fixed.** New `fernjo_import::limits` (`cut_chars`, `clean`, `MAX_MESSAGE_CHARS` = 256, cut on character boundaries). `backup::open` strips control characters from `createdAt` and `app` and cuts them to 256 characters (they reach the webview in `BackupSummary` and `RestoreSummary`); the mRemoteNG `unsupported` message and the core's `DocumentError::Invalid` text in a JSON import are cut the same way. Test `long_backup_header_strings_are_cut_in_reports`.
2. *Export-target guard covers only the data folder:* **fixed.** `ensure_export_target_allowed` now refuses a path inside the data folder, the config folder (`settings.json`), the device folder and a pending data folder (`pending_data_dir`), all with `export_target_not_allowed`, resolved like before (symlinks and `..` through `resolved`). With `FERNJO_DATA_DIR` the config folder is the real `%APPDATA%\fernjo`, which is refused too. Test `export_refuses_config_device_and_pending_folders`.
3. *JSON and backup import do not validate control characters or length:* **fixed.** `limits::sanitize_nodes` applies the mRemoteNG caps to both paths: names 256, descriptions 4096 (newline, carriage return and tab kept), hosts 256 and emptied (not cut into another host) when longer; control characters are removed (a name that would become empty is called `unnamed`). The JSON import reports the count in an aggregate line; the backup restore changes the values silently (a backup made by fernjo never needs it). The `MAX_*_CHARS` constants stay in `mremoteng/file.rs` and are shared, not copied. Tests `json_import_strips_control_characters_and_caps_lengths`, `backup_tree_text_is_capped_like_the_other_importers`.
4. *`import_apply` holds the pending lock across disk work while the page-load hook takes it:* **fixed.** `AppState::clear_pending` uses `try_lock` and skips while the lock is held. Skipping is safe: `import_apply` clears the slot itself on success (and when a backup is never applicable); `import_inspect` holds the lock only to read and to swap the value, never across I/O, so a page load during a pick or inspect still clears. A poisoned lock is still recovered. Residual: an apply that fails keeps its pending value, and a reload that happened during that apply does not drop it; the next `import_begin` replaces it and the UI has no way to apply it without one. Test `clear_pending_does_not_block_while_an_apply_holds_the_lock`.
5. *Residual unzeroised non-secret copies:* **accepted.** They are not secrets.
6. *Restore confirmation could name more than counts:* **moved back to the backlog** ("Restore confirmation wording"): UI wording, no safety effect.
7. *Vault equal-generation divergence (follow-up 2):* **accepted.** Two histories that reach the same generation cannot be told apart without a per-device history; the generation check still catches every rollback.
8. *Startup notice when `settings.json` holds a `dataDir` that contains the device-local folder (follow-up 3):* **fixed.** New `Notice::DeviceFolderInsideDataDir { path }` (serialised `deviceFolderInsideDataDir`), produced in `AppState::build` by `device_folder_notice` only when the data folder came from `settings.json` (with `FERNJO_DATA_DIR` the device folder is `<data>/.device` by design; the default data folder is the config folder). `NoticeBar` shows it. Tests `startup_notice_when_data_dir_contains_the_device_folder`, `notice_serialises_as_device_folder_inside_data_dir`, UI test "tells that the device folder lies inside the data folder".

## 2026-11-19 – M7: session end event
**Context:** M7 closes a tab by itself when the user leaves the shell on purpose (`exit`, Ctrl+D). Whether an end was deliberate cannot be read off the final message text, and a shell that dies at once (a broken profile script, a refused login shell) must keep its tab and its message. Addendum to "M4 session IPC".
**Decision:** The `disconnected` state event carries `exitStatus` (the remote shell's exit code, `null` without one) and `endedCleanly`; both are `null`/`false` on every other event. The verdict lives in Rust (`classify_end` in `src-tauri/src/sessions.rs`): clean means exit status 0 after a session that was connected for at least `MIN_AUTO_CLOSE_SESSION` (5 s, measured from the moment the phase becomes `connected`). A non-zero status, no status, a lost connection, a close request and a failed connect attempt are never clean. The preference "close the tab when the shell ended cleanly" is `TerminalPrefs.close_on_exit` (unset means true, `DEFAULT_CLOSE_ON_EXIT`), carried by the existing `TerminalSettingsInfo`/`TerminalSettingsInput` (no new command, so the three-places rule and the no-secret table are unchanged; an input without the key means true). The UI applies the preference.
**Rationale:** The rule "what counts as a clean end" is logic and belongs in Rust, testable without a UI; the UI only reports the verdict to the user's choice. Keeping the preference out of the session means it applies live to sessions that are already open, instead of being captured when each session starts.
**Rejected alternatives:** Rust closing the tab itself (it would need the setting per session and would race the UI's tab state); the UI parsing `detail` (fragile text); treating every exit 0 as clean (a shell that exits within seconds is far more likely a fault than a decision).

## 2026-11-19 – M7: WebView2 background connection

**Context:** The v0.1.0 security review (finding 5) saw the WebView2 runtime, not `fernjo.exe`, open one HTTPS connection to a Microsoft address about 60 s after start while the app sat idle; `--disable-background-networking` had not stopped it. The README says fernjo's own code makes no connections, so the open question was what the runtime does and whether a setting turns it off.
**Measurement (Windows 11, WebView2 runtime 157.0.4341.60 (`pv` of the EdgeUpdate client key), release binary from `pnpm tauri build --no-bundle`, scratch `FERNJO_DATA_DIR`, no DevTools port):** a script started `fernjo.exe`, found all descendants through `Win32_Process` parent ids (so other WebView2 apps on the machine do not count), and sampled `Get-NetTCPConnection` and `Get-NetUDPEndpoint` about every 0.7 s, logging each new non-loopback connection with its time and the process type from the command line. Result without a candidate switch (three runs, one of them with the `--log-net-log` flag): exactly one connection, `150.171.27.11:443` (Established), first seen at 62 s, 62 s and 61 s, from the `utility` process with `--utility-sub-type=network.mojom.NetworkService` in the two runs that logged the type; no UDP endpoint and no other remote address in 100 to 180 s. The address is one of the two A records of `edge.microsoft.com` (CNAME chain `edge-microsoft-com.ax-0002.ax-msedge.net`, `150.171.27.11` and `150.171.28.11`, resolved the same day); it has no reverse DNS entry, so the link is by the forward lookup only. The run with the `--log-net-log` flag recorded exactly one URL request in 100 s: `POST https://edge.microsoft.com/componentupdater/api/v1/update`, the Chromium component updater (it checks for updates of components the runtime downloads separately from its own binaries). Request bodies were not inspected.
**Candidates (one at a time through `WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS`):** `--disable-background-networking` (100 s): connection at 61 s, no effect, as in the review; `--disable-component-update` (100, 180 and 180 s): no connection in any of the three runs; `--disable-field-trial-config` (100 s): connection at 61 s, no effect; the four switches `--disable-background-networking --disable-component-update --disable-field-trial-config --disable-sync` together (100 s): no connection, which fits the component updater being the only cause in this window. Microsoft's page "WebView2 browser flags" explains the mechanism (`WEBVIEW2_ADDITIONAL_BROWSER_ARGUMENTS`, `AdditionalBrowserArguments`) and warns that production apps should not rely on flags because they may be removed or altered; it does not list `disable-component-update` or `disable-background-networking`. They are Chromium switches that this runtime version honours, which only the measurement shows. `log-net-log` is on the documented list.
**Decision:** `app.windows[0].additionalBrowserArgs` in `src-tauri/tauri.conf.json` is `--disable-features=msWebOOUI,msPdfOOUI,msSmartScreenProtection --disable-component-update`. Setting the key replaces wry's own default (`--disable-features=msWebOOUI,msPdfOOUI,msSmartScreenProtection`, `wry` `webview2/mod.rs`), so the default is repeated in full. The test `webview2_arguments_keep_wry_defaults_and_turn_off_the_component_updater` in `src-tauri/tests/tauri_conf.rs` pins both parts. Verified on the built binary: the WebView2 browser process command line carries both switches (also next to the DevTools port variable, which adds to the configured arguments), the page loads (`document.title` is `fernjo`, URL `http://tauri.localhost/`), and a 180 s idle run showed no remote connection from any process of fernjo.
**Effects and limits:** the switch only affects the runtime instance that fernjo starts; Windows or Edge Update still update the installed WebView2 runtime itself (outside fernjo). The app loads only its own local page, so it does not depend on components the updater would fetch; a runtime that never updates those components in fernjo's profile is accepted for that reason. The key applies to Windows only. The measurement covers one machine, one runtime version and a few minutes of idle time: other background traffic later, on crashes (crash reports go to Microsoft unless custom crash reporting is on, see the Microsoft page below) or depending on the Windows diagnostic data setting cannot be excluded. macOS (WKWebView) and Linux (WebKitGTK) were not measured; they are owner checks (`lsof -i -n -P | grep -i fernjo` on macOS, `ss -tnp | grep -i fernjo` on Linux, in the roadmap block "Open manual checks (owner)").
**Rejected alternatives:** documenting the limit only (a one-switch fix exists and was verified); `--disable-background-networking` (measured: no effect); extra Chromium switches such as `--disable-sync` or `--disable-field-trial-config` (not needed, no effect on their own); blocking `edge.microsoft.com` in a firewall (outside the app); shipping a fixed WebView2 runtime (large, and the app would then own its security updates); SmartScreen changes (already off through wry's default and not the observed traffic).
**Sources:** Microsoft Learn, "Data and privacy in WebView2" (https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoft-edge/webview2/concepts/data-privacy: required diagnostic data is collected regardless of the Windows setting; SmartScreen and crash reporting are the features with APIs); "WebView2 browser flags" (https://learn.microsoft.com/microsoft-edge/webview2/concepts/webview-features-flags); WebView2Feedback issue 5047 (https://github.com/MicrosoftEdge/WebView2Feedback/issues/5047: an earlier, different background request, the browser network time, fixed in runtime 133.0.3065.69 and not what was measured here).

## 2026-11-20 – M7: close confirmation, batch move and panel layout

**Context:** three M7 mechanisms that the other entries do not cover.
**Decision:**
- *Close confirmation:* Rust intercepts `CloseRequested` of the main window (`on_window_event` in `lib.rs`). Only sessions in the `connected` phase count (`SessionManager::connected_count`, rule `needs_close_confirmation`); with none the window closes at once. Otherwise the close is prevented and a native dialog ("Close fernjo?", buttons "Close" and "Keep open") is shown without blocking the main thread; its callback closes every session and ends the window with `destroy`, which does not raise `CloseRequested` again. `AppState.close_prompt_open` stops a second dialog from stacking while one is open. No new command and no new wire event, so the three-places rule and the no-secret table are unchanged.
- *`Tree::move_nodes`:* duplicate ids are ignored; an id inside another listed id's subtree is skipped and travels with its ancestor; the moved nodes keep the order of `ids` and land contiguously. `index` counts among the new parent's children after all moved nodes are taken out (`index == len` appends). A target that is, or lies inside, any moved subtree refuses the whole batch (`InvalidMove`); depth and index are validated before the first node is taken out, so the move is atomic. Empty `ids` is a no-op.
- *Panel layout:* the sidebar width and the properties panel height live in `localStorage` under `fernjo.layout.v1` (view state only, Rust knows nothing about them). Each stored value is validated per field (finite number inside its range, otherwise the default for that field); the clamp to the current window size is for display only (`effectiveSidebarWidth`), so a transient small window never overwrites the stored value. A write happens only when the user drags or resets.
- *Restore and sessions:* `apply_backup` (`import_commands.rs`) closes the live sessions before `replace_tree`, and `replace_tree` can now be refused with `changed_on_disk` (fingerprint check). A refused restore therefore ends every session and restores nothing. Accepted: a restore replaces everything anyway, and a pre-check needs a new store API.
**Rejected alternatives:** an in-app close dialog (needs a new command and wire event for the same result); closing sessions only after `replace_tree` succeeds (needs the pre-check above); persisting the clamped layout value (a short visit to a small window would shrink the panel for good).
**Limits:** Cmd+Q on macOS left through `RunEvent::ExitRequested`, which was not intercepted in M7, so no confirmation appeared there; fixed in M10, see "M10 quit confirmation on ExitRequested" (still an owner check on the macOS laptop). How the non-blocking dialog behaves on macOS and Linux has not been seen (owner check). A `changed_on_disk` error from a vault credential delete or from a restore shows its text but no Reload button (backlog "Reload bar for changed_on_disk outside the tree").

## 2026-11-20 – Linux AppImage: offer an application menu entry

**Context:** the window of the AppImage shows a placeholder icon in the title bar on the CachyOS laptop (Wayland), after the v0.1.0 report and after the `set_icon` attempt of v0.1.1 (which changed nothing; `tao` already sets the embedded icon when it creates the window, so the second call was a duplicate). Under Wayland the program cannot hand its icon to the compositor (GTK 3 has no such protocol); the compositor looks the window's app id up in an installed `.desktop` entry. Tauri leaves `enableGTKAppId` off, so the app id is the program name, `fernjo`, and the entry inside the AppImage (`fernjo.desktop`, `StartupWMClass=fernjo`, `Icon=fernjo`) is only seen when something integrates the AppImage. The `.deb` installs it. This cause is **unverified**: no Linux desktop was available, and the earlier note that the taskbar icon is right is not explained by it.
**Decision:** `src-tauri/src/app_menu.rs`. On Linux, when `APPIMAGE` names the file and `FERNJO_DATA_DIR` is not set, the setup hook shows one non-blocking dialog "Add fernjo to the application menu?" with three buttons: "Add" writes `fernjo.desktop` (`Exec` quoted per the Desktop Entry specification, pointing at the AppImage's current path) and the 128x128 icon below `$XDG_DATA_HOME` (default `~/.local/share`), through a temporary file and a rename, icon first; "Never" writes `appmenu-never` into the device-local folder; "Not now" and closing the dialog do nothing, so the question comes back at the next start (this lets the user move the AppImage first). An entry that already says exactly what it would say now is not asked about again; an entry that names another path (the AppImage moved) is asked about again. The dialog text names the folder and the path. A `fernjo.desktop` that is a symbolic link (checked with `symlink_metadata`, never followed) is managed by the user, for example by a dotfile manager, so the dialog is never shown for it (re-review N4 of M11: since M11 the entry is read through `fernjo_fs::read_capped`, which does not follow a link, so such an entry would otherwise be asked about on every start, and "Add" fails on it because `write_atomic` refuses a non-regular target).
**Rejected alternatives:** writing the entry silently on first start (a home-folder side effect without consent); documenting manual integration only (the owner wants the question); a `gtk` dependency to set the GTK window's icon name (cannot be compiled or checked on the Windows development machine); removing the entry again from the app (the dialog tells where the two files are).
**Limits:** the pure parts are tested on every OS (`app_menu` tests); the dialog and its effect on the title bar are owner checks on a Wayland desktop with an unintegrated AppImage. The entry may only take effect after the next start of the window or a log-in, depending on the desktop. Moving the AppImage later breaks the entry's `Exec` until the next start asks again.

## 2026-11-20 – M8: IronRDP crate set, RDP test host and the `rsa` advisory

**Context:** Task 4 of M8 adds the RDP session (`fernjo-rdp`, crate `session.rs` and `connect.rs`) on IronRDP.
**Decision (crate set):** the single crates, not the `ironrdp` meta crate, from one generation, pinned exactly in the root `Cargo.toml`: `ironrdp-connector` 0.12.0, `ironrdp-session` 0.13.0, `ironrdp-tokio` 0.12.0, `ironrdp-tls` 0.3.0 (feature `rustls-ring`, so the TLS stack stays on `ring`, which the SSH side already uses), `ironrdp-cliprdr` 0.9.0, `ironrdp-displaycontrol` 0.10.0, `ironrdp-dvc` 0.10.0, `ironrdp-input` 0.9.0, `ironrdp-graphics` 0.11.0, `ironrdp-pdu` 0.11.0, `ironrdp-core` 0.3.1, plus `x509-cert` 0.3 for the certificate. The `ironrdp` 0.17.0 meta crate is an older generation (connector 0.10); the two do not mix. `cargo deny check` passes licences, bans and sources (everything MIT/Apache-2.0 or already on the allow-list).
**Decision (RDP test host):** an in-process `ironrdp-server` as the CI host is **not possible**: it belongs to the older generation, whose crates pin `picky =7.0.0-rc.25` and `aes-gcm =0.11.0-rc.4`; the client generation pins `picky =7.0.0-rc.26`, and `aes-gcm 0.11.1` is a hard requirement of `fernjo-import`, so Cargo cannot resolve both. Instead `crates/fernjo-rdp/tests/support` has an in-process fake server (X.224 negotiation selecting TLS, then a TLS handshake with a self-signed `rcgen` certificate) that covers everything up to and including the certificate decision, the order of the prompts (certificate, then user name, then password), the timeouts and the failure codes in CI. The steps after the certificate (NLA, the session, damage, close, clipboard) read `FERNJO_TEST_RDP_ADDR` (`host:port`), `FERNJO_TEST_RDP_USER`, `FERNJO_TEST_RDP_PASSWORD` and optionally `FERNJO_TEST_RDP_DOMAIN`; without the address they skip with a message, also in CI. That is an owner check: `FERNJO_TEST_RDP_ADDR=host:3389 FERNJO_TEST_RDP_USER=... FERNJO_TEST_RDP_PASSWORD=... cargo test -p fernjo-rdp --test connect`.
**Decision (advisory):** every IronRDP release pins `picky`, and `picky` pins `rsa =0.10.0-rc.18` (also its newest, 7.0.0-rc.29). That forces the whole workspace's `rsa`, including the one under `russh` (SSH RSA-key signing), from the patched 0.10.0 to that release candidate, which RUSTSEC-2023-0071 (Marvin attack, RSA private-key operations not constant time) flags. The owner decided on 2026-11-20 to accept it: `deny.toml` ignores `RUSTSEC-2023-0071` with this reason. RDP itself does no RSA private-key work in a password/NLA client; the SSH side does when the user logs in with an RSA key. Revisit at every IronRDP update; drop the ignore once a release no longer pins `rsa` to a release candidate.
**Rejected alternatives:** patching `picky` into a vendored copy with relaxed pins (it also pins `pkcs1 =0.8.0-rc.4` and more, so it grows into a fork of a crypto dependency); removing the `rsa` feature from `russh` (SSH would lose RSA keys and RSA-only host keys); the `ironrdp` meta crate 0.17.0 with `ironrdp-server` (does not resolve next to `fernjo-import`, see above).
**Limits:** TLS-only servers (no NLA) are accepted (`enable_tls: true`); a MITM downgrade is bounded by the pinned certificate. Superseded in M11: the bound holds only while a pin exists and is never replaced, and on the TLS-only path the password reaches whoever holds the accepted certificate; servers without NLA are now refused with `nla_required`, see "M11 RDP: NLA required, TLS-only servers refused". Colour depth 24 is passed through but unverified against a real server (IronRDP's own client only offers 16 and 32). Kerberos is not offered (NTLM inside CredSSP only).

## 2026-11-21 – M8: RDP render path spike (canvas, `putImageData`, Tauri channel)

**Context:** the roadmap bar for M8 is at least 30 painted frames/s while scrolling and dragging at 1920x1080 on every OS, with keystroke latency the owner finds acceptable. The spike (branch `spike/m08-rdp-canvas`, throwaway, never merged) has two halves: the webview path (`spike_stream` sends synthetic RGBA region messages on a Tauri channel, `SpikePage.svelte` paints with `putImageData` and acknowledges; modes `full` = 1920x1080 every frame, `scroll` = 1920x900 region, `drag` = 400x300 region; 1 to 3 messages in flight, 10 s per cell) and the headless `spike_bench` example (real IronRDP session against a Windows 11 VM, 10 s per phase, scripted load). Measured by the owner on 2026-11-21 on the Windows 11 PC, the CachyOS laptop (Wayland) and the macOS laptop (Apple Silicon); webview versions were not reported. IronRDP crates as pinned in the entry above (connector 0.12, session 0.13).
**Measurement, webview path (painted frames/s; in flight 1 / 2 / 3):**

| OS | full | scroll | drag |
|----|------|--------|------|
| Windows 11 | 18.3 / 27.8 / 29.7 | 20.3 / 33.9 / 36.5 | 260 / 387 / 484 |
| CachyOS (Linux) | 40.6 / 44.9 / 49.8 | 48.1 / 51.4 / 57.4 | 354 / 493 / 453 |
| macOS | 48.5 / 60.0 / 76.6 | 59.6 / 72.4 / 86.5 | 292 / 418 / 457 |

Throughput of the channel plus paint is 130 to 240 MB/s on Windows, 160 to 390 MB/s on Linux and 130 to 610 MB/s on macOS; paint cost per message is 0.02 to 0.46 ms on Windows, 0.22 to 1.5 ms on Linux and 0.29 to 2.0 ms on macOS.
**Measurement, `spike_bench` (messages/s, mean and p95 bytes per message, copy cost per message):** Windows scroll 1.6 / 1.8 MB / 8.3 MB / 0.21 ms, moves 40.3 / 0.95 MB / 2.3 MB / 0.10 ms, repaint 32.1 / 0.59 MB / 1.7 MB / 0.07 ms; Linux scroll 35.5 / 0.18 MB / 0.92 MB / 0.05 ms, moves 29.4 / 0.15 MB / 0.75 MB / 0.05 ms, repaint 24.8 / 0.17 MB / 0.80 MB / 0.05 ms; macOS scroll 17.9 / 0.29 MB / 1.6 MB / 0.07 ms, moves 0.1 / 0.03 MB / 0.03 MB / 0.01 ms, repaint 32.6 / 0.64 MB / 2.0 MB / 0.10 ms. All three connected with NLA to the Windows 11 VM at 1920x1080, so connect, CredSSP, the framebuffer and damage events work against a real host on every OS.
**Reading the numbers:**
- With one message in flight Windows misses the bar (scroll 20.3, full 18.3 frames/s); Linux and macOS pass. The plan's step (c), two messages in flight, brings Windows to 33.9 (scroll) and three to 36.5, and every other cell of Linux and macOS is above 48 at any depth. The synthetic `scroll` message is about 6.6 MB (a near full frame), while the real session's mean message is 0.03 to 1.8 MB, so the real load is lighter than the worst case measured. Steps (a) and (b) were not measured separately: damage merging (`Emitter`) and dirty-region copies are part of the session design anyway.
- A full 1080p repaint on Windows stays just under 30 frames/s even at three in flight (29.7). It is not one of the bar's modes (scrolling and dragging) and an RDP server rarely sends full frames that fast, but it is the known ceiling on Windows.
- The `spike_bench` lines for Windows scroll (1.6 messages/s) and macOS moves (0.1 messages/s) are not a pipeline limit: the scripted load produced few updates in those runs (cause not determined; the same load gave 35.5 and 29 to 40 messages/s elsewhere). They stay unexplained and are not used for the gate.
**Decision:** the gate passes on the frame-rate half. The canvas path with `putImageData` is kept; `createImageBitmap` and WebGL (step (d)) and a lower colour depth (step (e)) are not needed. The transport constant for Task 10 is **three messages in flight** (`FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT = 3`): Windows at two passes by only 13 per cent in the worst case, three gives it a margin and costs nothing when the server sends less than the channel carries; at saturation it adds at most about one frame time of queueing, so lower it if the real session feels laggy.
**Latency (open):** the owner cannot judge keystroke latency from the spike, because the spike page does not show a real session (the benchmark is headless). It was therefore not judged. The bound from the numbers is one to three queued paints at 17 to 55 ms each at saturation (about 30 to 80 ms) and no queueing below it; the judgement happens in the first real RDP tab and is an owner check at the end of M8 (typing in a real session on each OS). The roadmap criterion "owner judges keystroke latency acceptable" therefore stays open.
**Limits:** one run per cell, no warm-up discarded, synthetic content (random-looking pixels, so no compression effects), webview versions not recorded, the load of `spike_bench` partly ineffective (above). Clipboard, resize and reactivation were not exercised by the spike.
**Rejected alternatives:** a native render surface (wgpu or softbuffer over the tab area) now (the bar is met on the canvas path; the scope-change task stays unused); WebGL texture upload (no need, the paint cost is under 2 ms per message everywhere); one message in flight (Windows misses the bar).

## 2026-11-21 – M8 settings and schema 3

**Context:** RDP connections need a domain, a resolution, a colour depth and a clipboard switch, set per connection or per folder like the SSH settings.
**Decision:** four flat optional fields on `Settings` (`domain`, `rdpResolution`, `rdpColorDepth`, `rdpClipboard`) and the same four on `ResolvedNode`, inherited node, then folders upward, then the default (`fit`, 32 bits, clipboard on; the port default for RDP is 3389). `rdpResolution` is `{mode:"fit"}` or `{mode:"fixed",width,height}`; colour depth is one of 16, 24 and 32; the domain is at most 256 characters. `connections.json` is `schemaVersion` 3 with a no-op migration `two_to_three` (the new fields are optional, so a v2 file loads unchanged and is written as v3); a stored value that is not valid (a depth of 8, a fixed size outside 200 to 8192, an overlong domain) is dropped on load, not refused. The connect plan (`connect_plan::resolve_session`) turns the effective values into an `RdpPlan`: the credential's domain beats the connection's, a blank value counts as unset, the connection's own user name beats the entry's, and a private-key credential on an RDP connection is `MissingReason::WrongKind` (RDP takes a password). The mRemoteNG import maps `Colors` and `Resolution` and leaves a value that mRemoteNG inherits unset; everything else about the display (redirection, gateway, scaling) is still reported as not carried over.
**Rationale:** flat optional fields keep a v2 file readable by the new code and the inheritance rules identical to the existing settings; dropping an invalid stored value matches how the other settings treat a hand-edited file, and the UI never shows a value Rust would refuse.
**Rejected alternatives:** one nested `rdp` object (a second inheritance rule for objects); refusing a file with an invalid value (a typo in a synced file would lock the user out of every connection); a real migration step (nothing to rewrite).
**Limits:** colour depth 24 is passed through but untested against a real server; a connection whose resolution is fixed does not follow the tab (the canvas scales, see "M8 session IPC").

## 2026-11-21 – M8 session IPC

**Context:** an RDP tab is a desktop, not a byte stream, so the terminal's output path (batches of bytes, acknowledged by length) needs a second shape, and it should reuse the session manager instead of forking it.
**Decision:** the wire contract is documented at the top of `src-tauri/src/sessions.rs` ("RDP sessions"). The raw message of an RDP session is a region: `u16 x, y, width, height` (little endian) and `width * height * 4` RGBA bytes, copied from the session's framebuffer when it is sent, so the newest pixels win. Two more JSON events: `desktop` (before the first region and on every size change) and `clipboard`. `open_session`, `session_resize` and `reconnect_session` carry pixels in `cols` and `rows`; input is `rdp_input` (at most 256 events per call, `too_many_events` above that) and clipboard text `rdp_clipboard`, both in `src-tauri/src/rdp_commands.rs` and counted in the three-places rule and the no-secret table. The code lives in `src-tauri/src/sessions/rdp.rs`, a child module of `sessions`, so it shares the manager's private `Shared`, `Op` and `Entry` without widening their visibility. The forwarder keeps the union of the damage rectangles while the UI is behind and sends one region per available credit; the credit is a `FlowControl` of two whole frames that resumes as soon as one acknowledgement brings the count back to that level, so up to three whole-desktop messages are in flight (`RDP_FRAMES_IN_FLIGHT`, the number the spike needs on Windows, "M8: RDP render path spike"), recreated on every desktop size change. Sizes are clamped to 200 to 8192 and rounded down to an even number, and a zero side is never sent. An RDP session never ends "cleanly": there is no exit status, and the tab stays with its reason and Reconnect. The prompts reuse the existing ones; the two new views are `certificate` and `certificateChanged`, answered with the host key answer shapes.
**UI:** `ui/src/lib/rdp/` (`keymap.ts`: scancodes from `KeyboardEvent.code`, never `key`; `pointer.ts`; `controller.ts`: painting with `putImageData`, input batched to one send per animation frame with key events at once, the debounced resize; `clipboard.ts`; `RdpView.svelte`). The store reads the connection's effective resolution mode and clipboard setting with `get_node` when the canvas attaches (the clipboard is off until it is known). In remote focus `routeKey` takes only full screen (Ctrl+Shift+F11) and the tab switch (Ctrl+Tab, Ctrl+Shift+Tab); every other chord, Ctrl+Shift+W included, reaches the remote machine. Full screen is in-app (sidebar hidden with CSS so the tree keeps its scroll position, tab strip unmounted) and ends with the session, with any change of tab and with the chord.
**Rejected alternatives:** a native render surface (the spike met the bar on the canvas); a second channel for pixels (Tauri orders raw and JSON messages on one channel already); counting messages instead of bytes for the credit (needs the acknowledgement to name the message); keeping a pending damage rectangle when the session closes (the last frame of an ending session may not be painted, accepted).
**Limits:** the UI was checked in jsdom with mocked controllers only; the real canvas, the resize observer and the pointer mapping run in no browser until the owner's check. Mouse coordinates are clamped to the desktop in `fernjo-rdp` (`Session::input`), not again at the command.

## 2026-11-21 – M8 RDP certificate pinning

**Context:** RDP servers almost always present a self-signed certificate, so the usual TLS chain check would refuse every server; the trust decision is the user's, like an SSH host key.
**Decision:** the SHA-256 of the DER certificate, shown as `sha256:<lowercase hex>`, is pinned per `host:port` (host lowercased) in `<device folder>/rdp_certs.confirmed` (`CertStore` in `crates/fernjo-rdp/src/cert_store.rs`; one line per pin; a capped read, an atomic write, a process-wide lock around read-modify-write; memory for the run when the device has no folder). The certificate is decided after the TLS handshake and before any credential is sent: unknown asks (`certificate`: "Trust once", "Trust always", "Cancel"), a match connects silently, a different pin blocks with `certificateChanged` ("Cancel" has the focus, "Replace and connect" rewrites the pin); a cancel, a wrong answer shape or a closed tab is a rejection (`certificate_rejected`). User name and password prompts, when the plan has no value, come only after the certificate was accepted. The pins are device-local on purpose (like `known_hosts.confirmed`): a pin in a synced folder would let another device's answer decide for this one.
**Limits:** TLS-only servers (no NLA) are accepted (`enable_tls`), so a man in the middle who strips NLA gets as far as the certificate prompt; the pin bounds it. Superseded in M11: servers without NLA are refused with `nla_required` before TLS, see "M11 RDP: NLA required, TLS-only servers refused". Kerberos is not offered. A certificate that is valid for a different name is not checked against the host (the pin is the check).
**Rejected alternatives:** validating against the system trust store (fails for the common self-signed case); trust on first use without asking (a first connection to a hostile server would be silent).

## 2026-11-21 – M8 RDP TLS handshake signature check

**Context:** the security review of the v0.2.0 release candidate found that `ironrdp_tls::upgrade` (ironrdp-tls 0.3.0) defaults to `CertificateValidation::DangerouslyAcceptInvalidCertificate`, whose verifier also returns success for the TLS handshake signatures. The pin of "M8 RDP certificate pinning" is the SHA-256 of a public certificate, so a server (or a man in the middle) that replays the real certificate without holding its private key passed the pin. With a TLS-only server that sends the user name and password in clear, with NLA it exposes the NTLM response to offline cracking.
**Decision:** `connect.rs` upgrades with `ironrdp_tls::upgrade_with_certificate_validation_callback_for_endpoint` and a callback that accepts every chain, name and expiry failure (self-signed is the normal case; the pin decides afterwards). The strict verifier underneath still checks the handshake signature against the certificate's public key, so the peer has to hold the private key. Test: `a_server_that_cannot_sign_for_its_certificate_fails_the_handshake_before_any_prompt` (fake server `Behaviour::ImpersonatedTls`: real certificate, another key) expects `tls_failed` and no prompt.
**Limits:** the strict verifier loads the platform root store and refuses to start when it holds no usable root (a Linux machine without `ca-certificates` gets `tls_failed` for every RDP connection). The fix is verified against the in-process fake server (ECDSA self-signed certificate) only; a Windows host's RSA certificate is an owner check (`cargo test -p fernjo-rdp --test connect` with `FERNJO_TEST_RDP_ADDR`). `ironrdp-tls` also sets a `KeyLogFile` (TLS secrets are written when `SSLKEYLOGFILE` is set in the environment; local only, accepted).
**Rejected alternatives:** an own `ServerCertVerifier` that skips the chain but checks signatures through the crypto provider (no dependency on the root store, but a copy of the upgrade code; adopted in M11, see "M11 RDP TLS: own verifier, strict handshake signature"); keeping `upgrade` and relying on NLA (TLS-only servers are accepted, see "M8 RDP certificate pinning").
**Known and not fixed (from the same review, owner to accept or schedule, see the backlog):** a hostile server can make the client buffer unbounded data on a static channel (ironrdp-svc reassembly has no cap; the 1 MiB clipboard cap applies after reassembly); the clipboard setting is on by default and syncs the local clipboard on focus.

## 2026-11-21 – M8 clipboard path

**Context:** the remote clipboard is text only (CLIPRDR with `CF_UNICODETEXT`), both ways, and the webview decides what `navigator.clipboard` allows.
**Decision:** Rust (`fernjo-rdp`, `TextClipboard`) converts line ends (CRLF on the wire, LF locally), caps the text at 1 MiB and never echoes text back to where it came from; with the connection's clipboard setting off the session offers no clipboard channel. The UI (`ClipboardSync` in `ui/src/lib/rdp/clipboard.ts`) writes what the remote side copied with `navigator.clipboard.writeText`, and offers the local clipboard with `readText` (1.5 s timeout) when the desktop gets the focus and before a paste chord (Ctrl+V, Cmd+V on a Mac, Shift+Insert) reaches the remote machine: the paste key and every key after it wait until the read settles, so the remote side pastes what was copied here, and are dropped when the focus is lost meanwhile. A refused or timed-out read or write shows one line, "The clipboard is blocked by the web view". Text that did not change, is empty or is over 1 MiB (UTF-8 bytes) is not sent.
**Open:** which mechanism each web view allows is **not verified** (WebView2 shows a permission prompt for `readText` and keeps the promise pending until the user answers, see "M4 clipboard path"; WKWebView and WebKitGTK were not tried). It is an owner check on Windows, macOS and Linux. If `blocked` shows up on a platform the fix is native clipboard commands (backlog "Native clipboard commands", which needs a decision on the `arboard` licence), or reading the clipboard from the native `paste` event instead of `readText`.
**Rejected alternatives:** files and images (not in M8's scope); polling the local clipboard (permission prompts and wasted reads).

## 2026-11-21 – M8 whole-branch review: fixed and accepted

**Context:** a fresh reviewer read the whole M8 diff; the findings were checked against the code before anything was changed.
**Fixed:** (1) a user name typed as `DOMAIN\user` together with a configured domain made the login layer (sspi) refuse the pair with `MixedFormat`, shown as "the server broke the RDP protocol": the configured domain is no longer passed when the user name contains a backslash (`build_params`); (2) `pnpm test` exited with code 1 because a rejection in the success handler of `SessionStore#loadMode` escaped its second `then` argument (the mock answered without the RDP fields): it is a `catch` now and reads the fields defensively, so the evidence in the roadmap is a command result; (3) a server that goes quiet during a reactivation could neither be closed nor time out: the reactivation read is bounded (30 s per step) and ends on `close`; (4) a key whose release looked like the app chord Ctrl+Tab (hold Tab, press Ctrl, release Tab) stayed down on the remote machine: a key that went down always gets its release; (5) a resize asked for while the certificate or password prompt was open was dropped (no display channel yet): the view compares its box with the desktop again once the session is live; (6) the desktop is capped at 8192 by 4320 pixels in area (`MAX_DESKTOP_PIXELS`), because each side alone allowed 256 MiB of framebuffer plus the decoded image and up to three region messages of that size; (7) `rdp_clipboard` ignores text above 1 MiB instead of queueing it.
**Accepted, not changed:** the TLS socket idles while a person answers the prompts (a server with a short pre-authentication timeout then fails after the answer; unverified without a real server); remote clipboard text is written to the local clipboard without a confirmation and without a rate limit (the setting is per connection and on by default, as designed; a hostile server can overwrite the local clipboard and send 1 MiB texts repeatedly); the password sits in IronRDP's own non-zeroized `String`s (its API takes them); the domain is a normal setting stored in `connections.json` and shown in the UI, only the password is secret (the plan's wording that the domain never reaches the webview was wrong); cancelling the user name or password prompt reports `auth_failed` ("the server refused the login") although nothing was sent; an invalid domain on a vault entry falls back to the connection's domain; a fixed resolution is rounded down to even values; Caps Lock and Num Lock state is not synchronised; `toDesktop` rounds instead of flooring; the `SENTINELS` list of the no-secret test does not include the domain sentinel, and its `opened_rdp` helper dumps the events before the failed connect has run, like the SSH helper does.
**CI follow-up (pull request 22):** `cargo deny check` on the Linux runner reported two more unmaintained notices that only exist on non-Windows targets and came with IronRDP's `sspi`: `atomic-polyfill` (RUSTSEC-2023-0089, through `winscard`, `iso7816`, `heapless`) and `pin-utils` (RUSTSEC-2026-0441, through `async-dnssd`). They are notices, not vulnerabilities, and `sspi` offers no upgrade, so `deny.toml` ignores them with reasons like the other unmaintained entries. CodeQL flags six test-only sentinel passwords in `fernjo-rdp` and `sessions/rdp/tests.rs` as hard-coded credentials (not a required check; to be dismissed as "used in tests" by the owner, like the earlier ones).

## 2026-11-21 – M8 first owner run: lag, resize and the clipboard banner

**Context:** the owner ran the first real RDP sessions on Windows 11, macOS and the CachyOS laptop (all with `pnpm tauri dev`, against a Windows 11 host): connect, certificate prompt, NLA, typing, full screen and colour depth 24 worked; scrolling and dragging felt laggy on all three; resizing the window did not change the desktop; Windows showed "The clipboard is blocked by the web view" although text could be pasted.
**Decisions:**
- *Lag:* the likely cause is that `pnpm tauri dev` builds Rust unoptimised, and decoding the RDP stream (bitmap and RemoteFX tiles, colour conversion, TLS records) is a hot loop; the render spike was measured with release builds. The dev profile now optimises the `ironrdp-*` crates, `rustls` and `fernjo-rdp` (root `Cargo.toml`, like `argon2` and `fernjo-import`). **Not proven:** it was not measured against a server; if a release build (`pnpm tauri build --no-bundle`) is laggy too, the cause is elsewhere (the next suspects are the size of the damage union per message, the server's codec choice and the performance flags, which are the IronRDP defaults).
- *Resize:* `ActiveStage::encode_resize` returns nothing until the server's display control capabilities have arrived, and the session dropped the request silently then. The session now keeps the latest request and retries it after every message until it goes out (60 s at most). **Not proven** to be the cause the owner saw: a connection whose resolution is fixed (also an imported mRemoteNG one) never follows the window by design, and a server without display control keeps its size.
- *Clipboard:* the banner came from the `readText` call on focus, which WebView2 answers with a permission prompt (or never). A paste chord (Ctrl+V, Cmd+V, Shift+Insert) is now left to the browser and its `paste` event hands the text over without any permission (`ClipboardSync.offer`); the key waits up to 300 ms for the event and reads the clipboard instead when none comes. The focus sync still reads, but a refused read is silent; the banner only appears when writing the remote text locally is refused. The "M8 clipboard path" entry above describes the earlier read-based design.

## 2026-11-22 – M9 multi-device sync: a self-hosted hub, one encrypted head, merge with conflict copies
**Context:** Milestone 9 must make connections and vault entries follow the owner between devices (gaming PC, CachyOS laptop, macOS laptop). The owner uses one device at a time, so devices are almost never online together, and fernjo itself must do the sync (no Syncthing, Nextcloud or iCloud folder). The owner can host a server and chose to, reachable only inside the home network behind Caddy (`tls internal`, `benjo.loc`), on a Debian or Ubuntu VM under rootless Podman. Full design: `Docs/superpowers/specs/2026-11-22-m09-multi-device-sync-design.md`.
**Decision:** fernjo syncs through its own small program, `fernjo-hub`, which stores one end-to-end-encrypted snapshot (the connection tree plus the `vault.fernjo` bytes) as the shared head, with compare-and-swap (`If-Match` on a rising `seq`) and the last 10 envelopes kept. The hub holds no key. The envelope is XChaCha20-Poly1305 under an HKDF-SHA256 key derived from a random sync key; the hub secret and the sync key sit in the OS keychain and travel in one copy-paste pairing code. Saving is local and never waits for the hub; sync runs on start, on focus, every five minutes, after a save and on close, and an unreachable hub is the calm state "offline, N changes waiting". Devices merge three ways (last synced snapshot, local, head): rules C1 to C10 for the tree and V1 to V5 for the vault; a conflicting field keeps the head's value on the original item and turns the local version into a "(conflict <device> <date>)" copy, an edit beats a delete, placement conflicts take the head's and are reported. A head older than what a device has already synced is refused (`hub_rolled_back`) and can be rebuilt from any device ("Re-seed"). Joining a device replaces its local data after a confirmation. `known_hosts`, `settings.json`, host-key and certificate confirmations, vault marks and window layout stay per device.
**Rationale:** A stored intermediary is unavoidable when devices never overlap. The hub is our own program, so a single head with compare-and-swap is simpler than one blob per device and as safe for the threat model (the hub can read nothing and forge nothing; it can only withhold or roll back, and rollback is detected). Snapshots are small, so whole-state three-way merges are cheaper and easier to test than an operation log. Conflict copies lose nothing without a blocking dialog in a background run.
**Rejected alternatives:** (1) No intermediary at all (iroh, BitTorrent DHT, Hypercore, p2panda, Tailscale Taildrop, magic wormhole, QR or USB): each needs two devices online together or a holder someone runs. (2) Free public networks as the only copy (Nostr relays, Veilid, Blossom): no retention guarantee for a credential vault. (3) Hosted local-first services and products (Anytype, Jazz, Evolu, Etebase, CloudKit): lock-in, account or licence problems, or no fit for the merge rules. (4) A user-owned cloud bucket or drive folder (R2/S3, Dropbox, OneDrive, GitHub): viable and close; the owner chose to self-host, and refusal of a stale write was not verified, so a spike would have been needed (the snapshot format would carry over). (5) One blob per device with version vectors: more states for no gain with a hub we write. (6) An operation log: compaction and per-operation checks for data of a few KB used by one device at a time. (7) CRDTs and timestamp-wins merges: overkill, and silent. **Residual limits, not defended:** a hub that shows different devices different histories, or holds back the newest head; edits made away from home reach the hub only while fernjo runs on that device inside the network.

## 2026-11-22 – M9 sync: vault conflicts, damaged local vaults and unrepairable trees

**Context:** the spec rule V4 (two sides changed the vault, with different vault ids) and three corners found while implementing the engine had no stated outcome.
**Decision:** (1) *V4:* when both sides changed the vault file and the vault ids differ, the run stops with `vault_conflict_different_vaults` before anything is applied or pushed, on every trigger, until the owner resolves it (for example by leaving sync and joining again on the device whose vault should go). Consequence: tree sync stops while the two sides differ, because the run is not split; this is the price of "no silent drop". If only one side changed the vault file it wins as in V1 even when the ids differ (a replaced local vault ends locked). (2) *Damaged local vault:* `inspect_file` runs on the local vault file before any apply or push, so a local file that does not parse stops the run with `vault_unreadable` even when the head's file would have replaced it; the app's own port adds `vault_unusable` for a vault the app already marked unusable. Nothing is pushed from a file that cannot be read. (3) *Unrepairable depth:* when no local move can be undone to bring a merged tree under depth 60 (a head reshuffle caused it), the merge fails with `snapshot_invalid`, permanently until someone moves nodes on a device; the head is not applied. (4) *Head order on a two-sided reorder:* when both sides reordered the same folder the head's order wins and local-only nodes are appended, with a notice naming the folder.
**Rationale:** every one of these refuses to guess; the owner can always see a stuck state in the status line and recover with the existing tools (leave and join, re-seed, an encrypted backup).
**Rejected alternatives:** merging the entries of two unrelated vaults (the data keys differ, so the entries would have to be re-encrypted under one: new crypto surface for a rare case); skipping the vault and syncing only the tree during a V4 conflict (a half sync hides the problem); repairing a too-deep tree by moving the head's nodes (the head is the other devices' truth).

## 2026-11-22 – M9 sync: notices, join and the hub's header parser

**Context:** details of how merge notices and a join behave that the design left to the builder.
**Decision:** (1) *Notices:* findings accumulate across the retries of one run (de-duplicated by equality); an attempt that applied nothing contributes none; the app keeps them in `SyncRuntime` until the user dismisses them (`sync_dismiss_findings`), also those of a run that ended offline after its merge was applied. Residual limit: a plain error after an applied merge (for example `hub_unauthorized` on the push) returns no findings, so that notice is lost; the merge is idempotent, so the next run creates no second conflict copy or duplicate node. Notices are not kept across a restart. (2) *Join:* `sync_join` closes every open session first (they use the data being replaced), replaces connections and vault, and a replaced vault of another id ends locked: unlock it with the sync group's master password. The device's old auto-unlock keychain item is orphaned, never deleted by sync. (3) *The hub keeps its own header parser* (`peek_header`, version 1 only) instead of depending on `fernjo-sync`, so the hub binary stays small and cannot be broken by client-side changes; the 64 MiB limit and the layout are pinned against the client in tests (`fernjo-hub` is only a dev-dependency, of `fernjo-sync` and `src-tauri`, never a normal one).
**Limits:** none of this was tried with two real devices; see `Docs/hub.md` "Not verified yet".

## 2026-11-22 – M9 final review: what changed and the known limits

**Context:** the whole-branch review of M9 found no critical issue and a short list of fixes; two findings are limits that stay.
**Decision:** (1) *V3 notice:* when both sides changed the vault file and this device's password wrap (KDF cost, salt, wrapped key; `FileInfo.password_wrap` in `fernjo-vault`) differs from the base's and from the head's, the head's header is kept as the spec says and a new finding `PasswordChangeDropped` (wire kind `passwordChangeDropped`, no secret) tells the user to change the password again; before, a password change made on one device while another edited an entry was reverted without a word. (2) A successful `vault_unlock` counts as a local edit for the runner (as `vault_unlock_older` already did), so a run that waited for the unlock starts after the five-second debounce instead of at the next five-minute tick. (3) The close push waits (inside the three-second budget, on a worker thread) for a background run that holds the slot and then pushes only if something still waits. (4) `sync_leave` clears the state and the base even when the keychain refuses to delete its item; the answer is state `off` with `error` `keychain_unavailable` naming the item (`fernjo` / `sync-<group id>`) to remove by hand. (5) `fernjo-hub serve` stops on SIGTERM and SIGINT and lets a running write finish (tokio's `signal` feature, enabled for the hub only).
**Known limits (not fixed):** (a) *Switching the data folder while paired:* sync keeps its base per device, not per data folder. Choosing a different, already filled data folder makes the other tree look like a mass deletion against the base, and the next run would push it. Leave sync before changing the data folder, then join again. (b) *Notice text after a lock:* the names of vault entries in a merge notice stay visible in the notice bar after the vault is locked until the user dismisses the notice.
**Rationale:** (a) needs a per-folder identity for the base, which is more design than a final fix wave; (b) the names are the same ones the sidebar shows for connections, and dismissing the notice is one click.
