**Context:** The v0.2.0 review rated the RDP clipboard default (`rdpClipboard` effective `true` for saved connections) a Medium finding: while the remote desktop has the focus, the local clipboard text is offered to the host, and the host can overwrite the local clipboard. The owner uses the clipboard daily and decided on 2026-11-24 to keep it on.
**Decision:** The default stays on for saved connections and off for quick connect. Remaining risk, accepted: the local clipboard reaches the host when the desktop gets focus, and the host can overwrite the local clipboard. Mitigations: the setting can be turned off per connection or folder (it is inherited), quick connect has it off, and since M11 a tab that shares the clipboard shows a 12 px clipboard mark (tooltip and `aria-label` "Clipboard shared with <host>", colour `--color-text-muted`, no new token) in `TabStrip.svelte`. `Tab.clipboard` is still off until the connection has been read; `Tab.host` is read in the same step (`#loadMode`). The restore confirmation in `ImportDialog.svelte` also says, only when sync is set up (`SyncStore.status` present and not `off`), that the restore replaces the data on every device in the sync group.
**Tests:** `an_rdp_tab_sharing_the_clipboard_shows_the_marker_with_the_host_in_its_tooltip`, `no_marker_on_an_ssh_tab_or_an_rdp_tab_with_the_clipboard_off`, `with_sync_on_the_restore_confirmation_says_it_reaches_every_device`, `without_sync_the_restore_confirmation_is_unchanged` (vitest only, not checked in the real app).
**Rejected alternatives:** default off (against the owner's decision); a prompt on every focus change (noise for a daily-use feature).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 SSH: a server that stops reading, and channels the server opens

**Context:** M11 review #22 (S2): after the login, a server that stops reading its socket (keeping the TCP connection open) made russh 0.65.1's session task hang in `flush_or_timeout` (`run_inner`), which without an `inactivity_timeout` waits forever and while it waits polls neither the keepalive timer nor its command queue. The tab stayed "connected" with a frozen terminal, `Session::write` blocked once russh's queue was full, and closing the tab leaked the socket, russh's task, `pump` (stuck in `handle.disconnect`) and the app's writer task: the app's `Op::Close` sits behind the hung write. Keepalive cannot see this: its arm is not polled while the write hangs, and before `Authenticated` it does not count at all. A server-started re-key followed by silence has the same effect (russh polls no commands while a key exchange is active). Review #23 (S3): `ClientHandler` overrode only `check_server_key`, so russh's defaults accepted every server-opened channel (session, x11, agent, forwarded and direct TCP/IP, forwarded and direct streamlocal) and kept each until the server closed it; a hostile server could grow memory without limit.
**Decision:** (1) The socket clone of the handshake guard (now `SocketGuard`, `crates/fernjo-ssh/src/session.rs`) is kept for the whole session: `pump` owns it in `Connection` together with the russh `Handle`. When the session ends (close, dropped `Session`, dropped events receiver, or the server ending it), `Connection::end` sends the disconnect and waits for russh's task to finish, both within `CLOSE_GRACE` (2 s), and then drops the guard, which shuts the socket down: the hung write fails and the task ends. The clone also keeps the TCP connection open after russh dropped its half, so it is always dropped at the end. (2) `pump` also ends when the events receiver is dropped (`tx.closed()`), not only when the next output finds nobody: the app aborts the connect task (which owns the receiver) when a tab closes, and that now ends the session even while the app's writer hangs in a write. (3) `Session::write` and `resize` race the write against `Session::alive`, an `mpsc` sender whose receiver `Connection` holds, so a write that waits for SSH window or room in russh's queue returns an error once the session ended (russh's window wait is never woken by a dead task). (4) `ClientHandler` refuses every `server_channel_open_*` with `AdministrativelyProhibited` (unknown types are already refused by russh); fernjo requests no forwarding.
**Known limits:** a frozen session is not detected automatically, as before M11: the tab keeps showing "connected" until the person closes it (closing now always ends it, see above). Accepted by the controller (M11 Task 18, fix round 1) and handed to the owner as a known limit.
**Tests:** `crates/fernjo-ssh/tests/hostile_server.rs` (an in-process russh server, no container): `closing_ends_a_session_whose_server_stopped_reading` and `dropping_the_receiver_ends_a_stalled_session` (the server stops reading after the shell starts, the client writes until its writes stall; after the close the `Closed` event arrives, the stalled write returns, and when the server reads again it finds the connection closed; on the old code no close event and no return, and with the guard's shutdown removed the server still found the connection open), `server_opened_channels_are_refused_and_the_session_keeps_working` (all seven channel kinds get `ChannelOpenFailure(AdministrativelyProhibited)`, the shell still echoes); `session::tests::client_config_sets_keepalive_or_none` pins that no `inactivity_timeout` is set.
**Rejected alternatives:** russh's `inactivity_timeout` (tried in the first fix round: `(keepalive_max + 1) × keepalive`, at least 180 s; dropped because russh restarts it only on a loop pass that is not the keepalive tick, and its clock (QPC-based `Instant` on Windows) keeps counting while the computer sleeps, so after a sleep longer than the limit both timers are expired at wake and a healthy idle session would end as `ConnectionLost` almost every time, where the keepalive logic counts one miss and survives; it also cut silent pre-login waits on prompts); a timeout on `write_ops`' write in the app (a server whose shell does not read its input, for example while `sleep` runs, legitimately holds the SSH window shut for as long as it likes); a watchdog of our own (it would face the same sleep problem and could not tell a stalled server from an idle one); accepting server channels and closing them at once (still costs a round trip each and keeps the loop busy; refusing is the protocol's answer).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 keyboard-interactive prompts name the server

**Context:** M11 review #39 (S5, P3): `SecretsPrompt.svelte` showed a server's keyboard-interactive round (its name, instructions and prompt labels) under fernjo's fixed title "Authentication required" without naming the host, so an accepted server could copy fernjo's vault or key-passphrase prompt word for word and collect the master password.
**Decision:** `fernjo_ssh::KiRound` carries `host` (the connection's configured host; `Session::connect` passes `params.host`, already checked by `validate_host`), and `PromptView::KeyboardInteractive` sends it to the UI (`host` next to `name`, `instructions`, `prompts`). The UI titles the prompt "Sign-in question from the server", leads with fernjo's own line "The server <host> asks:", puts the server's name, instructions and field labels together in one quoted block (left rule, `role="group"`, `aria-label` "Text from the server <host>"), and adds fernjo's note "Your answer goes to <host>. fernjo never asks for its vault password in this form." Only existing tokens (`--color-border-strong`, `--font-mono`, `--space-4`); `DESIGN.md` describes the layout. The server's `echo` flag still decides whether a field is masked (it is the server's question); the marking is what tells the user whose question it is.
**Tests:** `SecretsPrompt.test.ts` `keyboard_interactive_names_the_server_and_marks_its_text_as_the_servers` (a round imitating the vault prompt: title without "Vault", the "asks:" line, all server text and the field inside the marked block, the buttons outside, the note; failed on the old component) and `other_secret_prompts_have_no_server_block`; `SessionPrompt.test.ts` `session_prompt_picks_the_variant` (new title); `sessions::tests::ki_prompts_are_capped_in_count_and_length` and the event serialisation test (host in the view and on the wire); `tests/auth.rs` `keyboard_interactive_round_is_shown_and_answered` (the round from the OpenSSH container names the host). vitest only, not checked in the real app.
**Rejected alternatives:** refusing or flagging rounds that mention "vault" or "master" (easy to dodge with other words, and a real server may use them); forcing every field masked (a one-time code with echo is a legitimate request).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 SSH: the device's confirmed keys outrank the synced `known_hosts`

**Context:** M11 review #24 (S4): the M6 layer ("hosts confirmed on this device") took two of its inputs from the file it guards against. `Session::connect` built the offered host key types (`restrict_host_key_algorithms`) from the synced `known_hosts` only, `decide` never read `known_hosts.confirmed` when `known_hosts` had no key for the host, and the synced prompt's `previous` listed only confirmed keys of the presented type. Whoever can write the data folder plus a machine in the network path could therefore delete the host's lines (plain first-contact prompt) or add an ECDSA line ahead of the confirmed ed25519 one (the mild "came from another device" prompt with nothing previous); either way the changed-key warning was downgraded and a stored password went to the interceptor after "Connect".
**Decision:** (1) The offered host key types are the confirmed file's types first, then `known_hosts`' types (`stored_algorithms` in `session.rs`; an unreadable confirmed file adds nothing). (2) `ClientHandler` reads every key this device confirmed for the host and port (`KnownHosts::stored_keys`, any type). If there is any and the presented key is not among them, the person gets the changed-key prompt (`HostKeyChangedPrompt`, "The host key has changed", `old` = all confirmed fingerprints), both when `known_hosts` does not know the key (Replace writes it to `known_hosts` and confirms it) and when it does (Replace only confirms it on this device; `known_hosts` is not touched). The synced prompt remains only for a host this device confirmed nothing for, so its `previous` field could never be filled any more and was removed from `HostKeySyncedPrompt`, `PromptView::HostKeySynced` and `HostKeySyncedPrompt.svelte`. This is stricter than M6 also for a legitimate key change made on another device (the second device now sees the changed-key warning instead of the synced prompt with the old fingerprint); a changed key is what it is. (3) Added after the final M11 review (Minor 7): when `known_hosts` does not know the key but this device confirmed exactly the presented key, `decide` asks nothing and parks `TrustAction::Restore`, so the key is written back to `known_hosts` after the handshake (`known_hosts` only lost its line, by sync or a hand edit; a first-contact prompt there only trains people to click "Trust"). The write-back is best effort (re-review N1): a failed `known_hosts` write (read-only data folder, full disk) is ignored and the connect goes on, because this device already confirmed that very key; the writes after a user's decision (`Add`, `Replace`) still end the connect when they fail.
**Tests:** `handler::tests::a_host_this_device_confirmed_is_never_a_first_contact_even_when_known_hosts_lost_it` (confirmed ed25519, empty `known_hosts`, presented ECDSA: changed prompt naming the ed25519 fingerprint; before: first-contact prompt), `a_key_of_another_type_planted_in_known_hosts_is_a_changed_key` (an ECDSA line added to `known_hosts`: changed prompt; Replace confirms the ECDSA key next to the ed25519 one), `a_key_that_differs_from_the_confirmed_one_is_a_changed_key` (same type: changed prompt, `known_hosts` untouched; replaces `a_key_that_differs_from_the_confirmed_one_asks_with_previous`), `session::tests::the_confirmed_host_key_types_are_offered_first`, `handler::tests::a_key_this_device_confirmed_is_accepted_silently_when_known_hosts_lost_it` (point 3).
**Rejected alternatives:** filling the synced prompt's `previous` with confirmed keys of every type (keeps the mild wording for what is a changed key); refusing outright when the presented key's type was never confirmed (a server that adds a key type would lock the person out without a choice).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 RDP remote clipboard events: coalesced

**Context:** M11 review #44 (R4): `forward` in `src-tauri/src/sessions/rdp.rs` sent every `SessionEvent::Clipboard` straight to the webview as a JSON event, while region messages are credit-bounded (`credit_for`). IronRDP's cliprdr passes unsolicited format data responses on, so a hostile server with the clipboard on (the default for saved connections) can stream text events of up to 1 MiB of text each (about 6 MiB of JSON when control characters escape), and Tauri 2.12.7 parks every channel body of 8 KiB or more in `ChannelDataIpcQueue` until the webview fetches it. Whether a real webview falls behind far enough to matter is unconfirmed.
**Decision:** `forward` coalesces: the first text goes out at once, then at most one `clipboard` event per `CLIPBOARD_EVENT_GAP` (500 ms); text that arrives within the gap replaces the waiting one (the clipboard holds one value, so only the latest matters), and waiting text is still delivered when the session ends. One `Option<String>` and a deadline, a `sleep_until` arm in the forwarder's `select!`; no UI change. A person copying twice on the remote side within half a second sees the second text up to 500 ms later.
**Tests:** `sessions::rdp::tests::a_burst_of_remote_clipboard_text_reaches_the_ui_coalesced` (paused clock, `tokio` `test-util` as a dev-dependency feature of `fernjo-app`): 1000 texts in a burst produce two events, the first and the last, the second exactly one gap later; text waiting at the end is delivered. Fails on the old code with 1000 events.
**Rejected alternatives:** counting clipboard events against the region credit (the UI acknowledges only raw region bytes, so the credit would leak, or the UI would need a second acknowledgement); dropping clipboard events while text is waiting without sending the latest (loses the final value); a bounded queue of clipboard events (still delivers stale values).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 sync: an older head vault in a both-changed merge

**Context:** M11 review #29 (Y1): when both sides had changed the vault (V2), `Vault::adopt_merged` installed the head file's header and wrote the merged entries without comparing the head's generation with anything. A peer holding the sync key (adversary 4) could play back an older genuine `vault.fernjo` (its own copies or the hub history it can decrypt): the entries added since that file counted as "deleted on head" and vanished on every device, the vault was wrapped under the old master password again, a KDF raise was undone, and no `rolled_back` appeared because the merged save is above the mark. `Docs/sync-format.md` V5 claimed such heads were refused.
**Decision:** the reference point is the generation of the base file, not the floor or the device mark. `Engine::merge_vault` reads the base's and the head's generation with the data key and refuses a head below the base with `local_failed` code `rolled_back` before anything is merged, applied or pushed; `VaultChange::Merged` carries `base_generation`, and `Vault::adopt_merged(head, base_generation, entries)` refuses the same (`rolled_back`, nothing written) as a second line. Every save raises the generation and every file the hub has held since the base descends from it, so a genuine newer head is never below the base; the floor and the mark, by contrast, include this device's own saves since the base, so a head from a device that saved fewer times is legitimately below them (the existing test `adopt_merged_writes_a_generation_above_both_sides` pins that case, and fails with a floor check). V5 and the threat-model cells (adversaries 3 and 4) now say this. The locked case is unchanged (review #37): with only the head changed and the vault locked, `adopt_file` cannot read the body, writes the older file and the next unlock reports `rolled_back`; the threat-model cell was corrected to say so (test `a_locked_adopt_of_an_older_file_is_refused_at_the_next_unlock_by_the_device_mark`).
**Known limit:** the check stops a replay of an older genuine file. A peer that also holds the data key (it once unlocked the vault) can seal a new body at any generation; such a peer reads every secret anyway (assumption about adversary 4).
**Tests:** `adopt_tests::adopt_merged_with_an_older_head_is_rolled_back_and_writes_nothing` (the review's probe: old password and one entry, then a password change and two entries; the file, `.bak`, generation, mark and entries stay, the new password still opens it; failed on the old code with `Ok`); `engine.rs` `a_head_with_an_older_vault_than_the_base_is_rolled_back_when_both_changed_it` (the head carries the group's first file while B edited an entry: `local_failed(rolled_back)`, B untouched, nothing pushed, the current password stays; failed on the old code with a synced run) and `a_both_changed_merge_where_this_device_saved_more_often_than_the_head_still_merges`.
**Rejected alternatives:** comparing with the floor or the device mark as `adopt_file` does (refuses legitimate merges where this device saved more often than the head since the base, and sync would stay stuck); refusing only in the vault (the engine would still build and report a merge that cannot be applied).
**Update 2026-11-25 (final M11 review I2 and re-review N2): what the app offers.** A run that ends with a vault `rolled_back` is reported as state `rolled_back` with code `vault_rolled_back`, so the footer and Settings offer Re-seed, which pushes this device's vault unmerged. That is right only when the refused head is older than the local vault file. In one corner it is not: after "open the older version" whose best-effort re-stamp save failed, the local file is below the device mark, and a head that another device saved from it (newer than the local file, below the mark) is refused by `adopt_file`; Re-seed would push the older local vault over the newer head. So every refusal goes through `fernjo_sync::engine::refused_head(head, local)`: `rolled_back` (Re-seed offered) only when the head's generation is below the local file's, else the plain run error `vault_below_mark` (no Re-seed; UI text: save the vault once on this device, for example add or edit a credential, then sync again; the save writes a generation above the mark, so the next run merges the head when it was saved from the base (the `adopt_file` corner), or, when the head is also older than the base (the engine's V5 corner), refuses it again as `rolled_back` and offers Re-seed, which the user then confirms knowing what it replaces). The engine reads the local file's generation with the data key when its V5 check fires; `AppPort` uses the open vault's generation for `adopt_file` and `adopt_merged` (memory changes only after a successful save, so it is the file's). Tests: `engine.rs` `a_head_below_the_base_but_newer_than_the_local_file_is_vault_below_mark` and `only_a_head_older_than_the_local_file_is_rolled_back`, `sync_commands` `a_head_newer_than_a_local_vault_below_the_mark_is_not_offered_reseed` (the corner state is made by raising the device mark and attaching the store again, the same handle state as a failed re-stamp; it also shows that a credential save resolves it) next to `an_older_vault_in_the_head_offers_reseed_and_reseed_keeps_the_newer_local_vault`.

## 2026-11-25 – M11 sync: only the vault file the vault knows is pushed

**Context:** M11 review #30 (D2, Y2): `AppPort::read` took the `vault.fernjo` bytes straight from disk (`Vault::file_bytes` was a plain capped read) and `apply` compared disk with disk. A writer of the data folder (adversary 5) that replaced the file with an older copy or another vault's file was stopped locally by the save guard (`changed_on_disk`), but the next sync run read the planted bytes as "this device changed its vault" (V1 keeps the local side) and pushed them: locked peers wrote the older file, a foreign vault id replaced a peer's vault, unlocked peers stayed on `rolled_back`. `reseed` and `setup` pushed `local.vault.file` without even parsing it, so an unparsable file stopped every peer with `snapshot_invalid`.
**Decision:** `Vault::file_bytes` returns the file only when it still starts with the header the handle last read or wrote (`disk_header`, the save guard's rule) and is `changed_on_disk` otherwise, locked or unlocked. Sync has no other reader of the vault file, so `read` (and the compare in `apply`) refuse such a file and the run, a re-seed and a set-up end with `changed_on_disk`; nothing is pushed or stored. Lock and unlock (which re-reads the file) to take a deliberately replaced file on. `Engine::setup` and `Engine::reseed` also run the `inspect_file` check that `attempt` already ran (`check_local_vault`, `local_failed(vault_unreadable)`), so the engine never pushes a vault file that does not parse whatever the port returns. Both guards look at the header only: a planted file that keeps this handle's header but has a damaged body is still pushed, and the peers cannot decrypt it: an unlocked peer refuses it (`unreadable`), a locked peer writes it and its next unlock fails as damaged (the previous file stays in its `vault.fernjo.bak` and in the hub history). A denial of service until the next good push, not a rollback or a disclosure. The existing test `a_vault_file_that_cannot_be_read_ends_the_run_and_is_never_replaced` now expects `changed_on_disk` for its garbage file instead of `vault_unreadable`.
**Tests:** `adopt_tests::file_bytes_are_only_the_file_this_handle_knows` (an older copy, another vault's file and garbage are `changed_on_disk`, also locked; the handle's own file is readable again; failed on the old code); `sync_commands::tests::a_vault_file_planted_behind_the_vault_is_never_pushed` (real app state and hub: after the older copy is put back, `sync_now` and `sync_reseed` end with `changed_on_disk`, the hub head stays at 2, the other device still unlocks both credentials, and `sync_setup` on a device with a planted file uploads nothing; failed on the old code with a synced run); `engine.rs` `reseed_and_setup_never_push_a_local_vault_file_that_does_not_parse` (failed on the old code: the re-seed pushed).
**Rejected alternatives:** a second method (`file_bytes_checked`) next to the unchecked one (sync is the only caller, and an unchecked reader invites the same mistake again); comparing with the last pushed base (a legitimate local edit changes the file too; only the handle knows which file is its own).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 sync: the sequence number never wraps

**Context:** M11 review #48 (Y4): `prepare_push`, `attempt` and `reseed` computed `seq + 1` unchecked (a panic in debug builds, a wrap to 0 in release builds, whose profile has no `overflow-checks`), and the hub accepts any `seq` on the first write. A head at `u64::MAX` (a garbage body from anyone with the hub secret, or a valid envelope from a peer) made "Re-seed" push `seq` 0, which the hub refuses for ever.
**Decision:** `next_seq` (`checked_add`) in the engine; at `u64::MAX` the run (when it has something to push) and the re-seed end with the new error `SyncError::SeqExhausted` (code `seq_exhausted`, no UI wording, so Settings shows its message) before anything is applied or pushed. Recovery stays with the hub's admin (remove `head.env`); `Docs/sync-format.md` says so. No upper bound on the hub or the client: any bound only moves the wedge to the bound (a hostile writer puts the head exactly there), and the clean error is what matters.
**Tests:** `engine.rs` `a_head_at_the_highest_sequence_number_ends_runs_and_reseed_with_a_clean_error` (a valid head at `u64::MAX` is taken; a local edit then ends the run and the re-seed with `seq_exhausted`, nothing written or pushed; panicked with "attempt to add with overflow" on the old code).
**Rejected alternatives:** a bound such as `2^53` on hub and client (see above); a hub command to clear the head (new hub surface for a case that needs the admin anyway).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 sync: peer text in `snapshot_invalid` is cut

**Context:** M11 review #50 (Y5): `Snapshot::from_plaintext` put serde's message (which quotes the offending value) and `parse_document`'s reasons (which name the node) into `SyncError::SnapshotInvalid` unbounded; `sync-errors.ts` has no wording for `snapshot_invalid`, so the whole text went to Settings and the footer and was re-sent with every `sync-status`. Text-only rendering, so a slow UI, not an injection.
**Decision:** `snapshot.rs` builds the error through `error::snapshot_invalid`, which keeps at most `MAX_REASON_CHARS` (200) characters and marks a cut with an ellipsis, like the import report's caps. The engine's and the tree merge's own reasons are fixed texts and stay as they are.
**Tests:** `snapshot::tests::peer_text_in_a_snapshot_invalid_message_is_cut` (a 1 MiB `protocol`, a 1 MiB node name with port 0 and a 1 MiB string as `seq`: message and `Debug` under 1 KiB; the old code gave a message of 1 048 644 bytes), `error::tests::a_snapshot_invalid_reason_is_cut_at_200_characters`.
**Rejected alternatives:** a fixed text for every `snapshot_invalid` (the reason is the only hint of what is wrong with a head; 200 characters keep it).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 sync: plain `http://` only in debug builds

**Context:** M11 review #53 (Y7): `check_hub_url` accepted `http://127.0.0.1` and `http://[::1]` in every build, although `Docs/sync-format.md` called it test-only. Pointed at a loopback hub without the TLS proxy, fernjo would send the bearer hub secret to whatever process holds the port, for example another local user's while the hub is down.
**Decision:** `ALLOW_PLAIN_LOOPBACK = cfg!(debug_assertions)` in `client.rs`: a release build refuses every `http://` address ("the address must start with https://"), also in a pairing code; a debug build keeps loopback `http://` because the in-process hub of the tests (`fernjo-hub` as a dev-dependency of `fernjo-sync` and `fernjo-app`) speaks plain HTTP. `cargo test` and CI build tests in the debug profile; `cargo test --release` fails the sync tests that talk to a loopback hub (checked: the eight fake-server tests in `client::tests` fail there because the client is refused, which is the release behaviour). `Docs/hub.md` and `Docs/sync-format.md` say so. A same-machine hub without the proxy therefore needs HTTPS (Caddy on loopback) in a release build. Fix round 1: the app's placeholder client for the engine calls that never talk (`idle_hub` in `sync_commands.rs`, used by `sync_setup`, `sync_join` and the pending count of every status) was `HubClient::new` with `http://127.0.0.1:9`, which a release build now refused, so set-up, join and the status of a paired device failed there; it is now `HubClient::offline()` (no address, no TLS roots, every request `hub_unreachable` without touching the network). No other non-test code builds a client from a literal address. Test: `sync_commands::tests::the_status_of_a_paired_device_never_builds_a_hub_address` (a device paired with an https hub, no hub running: status `ok`, pending count 1; failed in `cargo test --release` before with `invalid_hub_url`) and `client::tests::an_offline_client_needs_no_address_and_never_talks`. In `cargo test --release -p fernjo-app sync` 21 tests pass and 26 fail, each of them one that starts the plain-HTTP loopback hub (debug-only by design).
**Tests:** `client::tests::plain_http_to_loopback_is_refused_where_it_is_not_allowed` (`check_hub_url_with(url, false)` refuses both loopback forms and accepts `https://127.0.0.1`, and the constant follows `debug_assertions`; failed before the parameter was honoured); `plain_http_to_a_non_loopback_host_is_refused` now expects loopback `http://` to work exactly when the constant allows it. Both pass in the debug and the release profile (`cargo test --release -p fernjo-sync --lib client::`).
**Rejected alternatives:** a runtime flag or environment variable the tests set (a release binary would then still carry a switch that turns it on); `cfg(test)` (does not reach the integration tests or the dependent crate's tests).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 sync: sibling ordering in linear time

**Context:** M11 review #54 (Y8): `Merger::order_siblings` placed every node only the secondary side has with a search for its predecessor plus a vector insert. When this device reordered a folder and the head adds many nodes there, the head is the secondary side: 99 000 head-only nodes took 46 s in a debug build (measured here; the review's estimate was unconfirmed), on every run of the sync thread.
**Decision:** the placement is the free function `place_secondary` in `merge/tree.rs`, which keeps the order as a list linked by id (`HashMap<NodeId, Option<NodeId>>` plus first and last), so an insertion after the predecessor, at the front or at the end is constant time; the result is unchanged, including the fallbacks of the old code (no predecessor: first; predecessor not listed: last; both sides reordered: appended). The loop that puts conflict copies after their originals still searches every parent's list for every copy (copies times nodes); copies come only from conflicts with nodes this device edited itself, so a peer alone cannot make that large.
**Tests:** `tree_tests::many_head_additions_in_a_locally_reordered_folder_merge_in_linear_time` (99 000 additions, exact order asserted, under 20 s; 46.5 s before, about 2 s after in a debug build), `linear_sibling_placement_matches_the_search_and_insert_placement` (5000 generated cases with repeated ids, non-members and the append mode against the old algorithm kept as the test's reference; it fails when one branch of the new code is changed), and the existing merge tests unchanged.
**Rejected alternatives:** a position map over the vector (every insert shifts the tail, still quadratic); building the result in one pass from the secondary side's runs (harder to show equal to the old order in all fallback cases).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 JSON files: never read through a `serde_json::Value`

**Context:** M11 review #28 (I2): every reader of a JSON file parsed it into a `serde_json::Value` before looking at its shape: `connections::plan` (import), `persist` (`connections.json` at every start and `Store::reload`), `settings.rs`, `backup::open` (the `connections` document), `Snapshot::from_plaintext`, the sync state files and `open-tabs.json`. A `Value` costs about 98 bytes per input byte for `[{"":0},…]` (one map per one-key object). Measured with a counting allocator: 4 MiB of that junk cost 412 to 450 MiB in the import, the core loaders and a snapshot, and 837 to 870 MiB in `sync-base.json` (199 to 207x: the old code parsed the snapshot in it twice); the review measured 3.1 GiB for a 32 MiB import, and a `connections.json` with the junk under an unknown key (it still loaded) extrapolates to about 6 GiB at the 64 MiB cap on every start. The snapshot case, unconfirmed in the review, is confirmed. The derived `Deserialize` of the internally tagged `Secret` (vault entries, backup entries) and of `Node` (`#[serde(flatten)]` plus an internally tagged `NodeKind`) and `RdpResolution` buffers every field as serde `Content` too: junk under an unknown key of a backup entry's secret cost 45x even after the `Value` was gone.
**Decision:** two passes, no `Value`. `fernjo_core::json::head` validates the whole document exactly as `from_slice::<Value>` would (it reads every value with `deserialize_any`, so serde_json checks UTF-8 inside strings and counts the nesting limit of 128 the same way; `IgnoredAny` does neither) and returns only the top-level fields the reader decides on (`schemaVersion`, `format`, `formatVersion`, `schema`; a repeated key: the last wins, as in a `Value`). The reader then deserialises the same bytes into its typed structs, which skip unknown fields with `IgnoredAny`. A nested document is borrowed as `&RawValue` (workspace `serde_json` feature `raw_value`, no new crate) and read by the same two passes: the backup's `connections`, the snapshot's `connections`, `sync-base.json`'s snapshot. The outer document is validated first, so the nesting limit still counts from the outer root and the depth edges (`the_depth_limit_leaves_a_margin_below_what_the_loader_accepts`) are unchanged. `Node`, `RdpResolution` and `Secret` implement `Deserialize` by hand: fields in any order, the other kind's fields ignored like the tagged impl did (a field read before `type` is held cheaply and checked once the tag is known), unknown fields skipped, the same error texts for missing fields and unknown variants. The backup's node count (`too_many_nodes`) counts `children` elements while reading the borrowed text. `persist::parse_document_bytes(&[u8])` is the reader; `parse_document(Value)` stays for documents already in memory (tests) and serialises to bytes first. The migration steps run on the typed document (`FileIn`); every schema step so far only added optional fields, so they are identities, and a future step that rewrites data reads the old shape into its own typed struct. serde_json's recursion limit stays active on the typed pass (the typed structs only descend through `deserialize_map`/`deserialize_seq`, which count it), so deep input cannot overflow the stack.
**Behaviour that changed:** a key repeated inside one object of a typed struct (a node, a setting, the document's top level, a state file) is now refused as a damaged file, as serde's derive refuses it; before, the `Value` step let the last one win (the backup and vault bodies were already typed and refused it). A connection whose `children` is an array of invalid nodes *before* its `type` key is refused (the tagged impl ignored it); fernjo never writes either.
**Tests:** `crates/fernjo-core/tests/memory.rs`, `crates/fernjo-import/tests/memory.rs`, `crates/fernjo-sync/tests/memory.rs` (counting global allocator, 4 MiB of `[{"":0},…]` as the whole file, under an unknown top-level key, in a node, in `rdpResolution`, in a settings value, in a backup body, its `connections` and an entry's secret, in a snapshot, its `connections` and `sync-base.json`; peak below 2x the input, 4x for the backup; before 98x to 207x, after 1.00x for the file readers (the file buffer itself) and 0.00x for the readers that get the bytes); `json::tests::head_agrees_with_the_value_parse` (syntax errors, trailing text, invalid escapes, a lone surrogate, control characters, the nesting edge at 127 and 128 levels, also under an unknown key, numbers out of range, `-0`, repeated keys: same answer and same error text as the `Value` parse), `head_refuses_invalid_utf8_inside_an_unknown_string_like_the_value_parse`; `model::tests` and `entry::tests` for the hand-written impls (any order, the other kind's fields ignored also with the wrong type, the derived impl's refusals, repeated keys). The existing suites pass unchanged except `persist::tests::migration_chain_has_a_one_to_two_and_a_two_to_three_step`, which now passes the typed document through the chain instead of a `Value`.
**Rejected alternatives:** the review's linear pre-scan with a container budget (a budget still lets `[0,0,…]` cost 16x as a `Value`, and a budget that fits every legitimate tree is far above what an attacker needs); typed structs without the first pass (a file of a newer schema could then fail on its shape before its version is read, and the nesting limit would no longer count the skipped parts); `Box<RawValue>` (copies the text).
**Addendum (accept/refuse differences of the hand-written impls, M11 review #28):** `Secret`, `Node` and `RdpResolution` are not byte-for-byte the old derived impls. Now refused, was accepted: the sequence forms of `Secret` (`["password","pw"]`) and `RdpResolution` (the tagged derive read the tag as the first element of a sequence); an integer `type` tag. Now accepted, was refused: a map-form tag (the tag as a one-key map). Now refused, was accepted: a repeated key of the other variant that appears before `type` (the tagged impl buffered it and ignored it; the hand-written one refuses every repeated key). fernjo never writes any of these forms. `NodeKind` no longer derives `Deserialize` (nothing read it; `Node` reads its own kind). Test: `a_secret_no_longer_reads_the_sequence_form_the_derived_impl_accepted` in `crates/fernjo-vault/src/entry.rs` (the sequence case used to sit, wrongly, among the cases "the derived impl refused").

## 2026-11-25 – M11 terminal paste: filtered at the paste boundary; a background RDP tab holds remote clipboard text

**Context:** M11 review #31 (P1): xterm.js 6.0.0 wraps pasted text in `ESC[200~`…`ESC[201~` without removing an `ESC[201~` inside it, and fernjo passed both the menu Paste and the keyboard paste unfiltered. Text from a hostile RDP server's clipboard (written to the OS clipboard by the RDP tab, even while hidden) or from any copied web text could end bracketed paste early and run the rest as typed input in an SSH tab.
**Decision:** `ui/src/lib/terminal/paste.ts` `sanitizePaste` drops both bracketed-paste markers and then every C0 control except TAB, LF and CR, DEL and the C1 range (U+0080 to U+009F); a marker re-formed by the first step loses its ESC in the second, so no ESC is ever pasted. Multi-line pastes keep their lines (xterm turns LF into CR as before). `TerminalController` is the single entry: the menu Paste and a `paste` listener in the capture phase on the mount element (ahead of xterm's own listeners on its textarea and element, `preventDefault` + `stopPropagation`) both call `#pasteText`. The filter lives in the UI because the keyboard paste never passes through Rust; `clipboard_read_text` stays a plain read (filtering there too would be a second copy that protects nothing the UI step does not). `RdpView.svelte` writes remote clipboard text only while its tab is visible and its canvas has the focus; otherwise the latest text is held and written when the canvas next gets the focus (before the focus offer of the local clipboard). Known limit: text the remote side copies just as the user switches tabs reaches the local clipboard only when that RDP tab is focused again. `fernjo_rdp::clipboard::from_remote` still keeps control characters; the paste filter covers every source.
**Tests:** `controller.test.ts` `menu_paste_cannot_end_bracketed_paste_early`, `a_native_paste_event_is_filtered_before_xterm_sees_it` (the event is taken before an inner listener sees it), `pasted_text_keeps_tab_and_line_ends_and_loses_other_controls`; `RdpView.test.ts` "a hidden rdp tab never writes the remote text locally; it waits for the tab's focus" and "a visible rdp tab whose desktop lost the focus holds the remote text too". Checked in vitest only (no WebView2 runtime on the development VM); whether `writeText` needs no gesture in the real app stays unconfirmed.
**Rejected alternatives:** asking before every multi-line paste (a prompt per paste; the filter already removes the breakout); xterm's `ignoreBracketedPasteMode` (would make every CR in a paste run a line).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 OSC 8 links go through `open_external_url`

**Context:** M11 review #58 (P2, W2): the `Terminal` had no `linkHandler`, so an OSC 8 hyperlink from the server used xterm's `defaultActivate` (a native `confirm()` with server-chosen text, then `window.open()`); only wry's missing new-window handler stopped the popup, and the URI never reached `check_external_url`.
**Decision:** `TerminalController` passes a `linkHandler` whose `activate` calls `preventDefault` and `deps.openUrl(uri)`, with `allowNonHttpProtocols: false`: OSC 8 links leave only through `open_external_url` like `WebLinksAddon` links, and xterm makes only http(s) OSC 8 links clickable at all.
**Tests:** `controller.test.ts` `osc8_links_go_through_openUrl_without_confirm_or_window_open` (failed before: no handler). Not tried in the real app with an OSC 8 sequence from the SSH test container (no WebView2 here).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 session close race: `closed` flag checked by `install_ops`

**Context:** M11 review #59 (P4, timing unconfirmed): `shut_down` sent `Op::Close` only when an op queue was installed. A connect task finishing in the same moment could install its queue and spawn the writer after that check; `abort` then stopped `run` at `forward`, and the writer, holding `Arc<Shared>` whose `ops` held the writer's own sender, kept the authenticated session alive with no entry left to close it.
**Decision:** `Shared.closed` (an `AtomicBool`) is set by `shut_down` while it holds the `ops` lock; `install_ops` (SSH and RDP), under the same lock, sends `Op::Close` and installs nothing when it is set, so the writer closes the session and ends, and no sender cycle exists. An entry is shut down only when it leaves the map, so the flag is never reset. The related window the review mentions (an `open_session` of the old page handled after `on_page_load` ran `close_all`) is not changed.
**Tests:** `sessions::tests::a_session_that_connects_after_its_tab_was_closed_is_torn_down` (close, then `install_ops` and a writer loop: the writer ends and the shared state is freed; before, the writer never ended).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 quick connect refuses `user:password`

**Context:** M11 review #60 (P5): `ssh://admin:secret@srv` parsed with the user `admin:secret`, which put the password into the tab title, `open-tabs.json` and the server's auth log.
**Decision:** `validate_quick` (so `parse_quick`, `quick_parse`, `open_quick_session` and a restored quick tab's reconnect) refuses a `:` in the user with the new code `quick_user_password` ("type only the user name (no :password); fernjo asks for the password"), shown inline under the quick connect field. POSIX user names cannot contain `:` and a Windows `DOMAIN\user` does not need it.
**Tests:** `quick::tests::refuses_a_password_typed_into_the_user`, the `password_user` case in `sessions::tests::a_malformed_hand_built_quick_target_opens_no_session`.

## 2026-11-25 – M11 RDP clipboard marker from the session's plan (`rdpSettings` event)

**Context:** M11 review #61 (P6): the tab's clipboard marker and RdpView's clipboard sync came from a separate `get_node` call with a `?? true` fallback that duplicated `DEFAULT_RDP_CLIPBOARD`; a sync apply or an edit between that call and the session's own plan made them disagree.
**Decision:** `rdp::start` sends `{kind:"rdpSettings", clipboard, host}` from the resolved `RdpPlan` before the attempt's first state event (open, quick open, reconnect, start after a vault unlock); the wire contract is in the doc comment of `sessions.rs`, section "RDP sessions". `SessionStore` sets `tab.clipboard` and `tab.host` only from it; `#loadMode` reads only the resolution. A tab without the event (a restored tab before its reconnect) has the clipboard off. No new command.
**Tests:** `sessions::rdp::tests::the_rdp_settings_event_carries_the_clipboard_setting_the_session_uses` (off, then on after an edit and a reconnect), `open_session_for_an_rdp_node_starts_an_rdp_session` (the event comes first); `sessions.rdp.test.ts` "the clipboard marker follows the session's settings event, not the connection" and "without a settings event the clipboard stays off even when the connection says on".

## 2026-11-25 – M11 `restart_app` asks like a close

**Context:** M11 review #65 (W5): `AppHandle::restart` replaced the process without `CloseRequested` or `ExitRequested`, so "Restart now" (after a data folder change) cut connected sessions without the "Close fernjo?" dialog and skipped `push_before_close`.
**Decision:** `restart_app` takes the `Window` and decides with `SessionManager::restart_action` (that is `exit_action(None, connected, prompt_open)`): with a connected session it calls `ask_to_close(window, connected, CloseThen::Restart)` ("Restart fernjo?", "N sessions are still connected. Restarting fernjo ends them.", "Restart" / "Keep open"; texts from `sessions::close_prompt`, the one `close_prompt_open` flag shared with the window close and Cmd+Q); without one it goes straight on. The restart goes through `sync_runner::finish_after_push` (which `destroy_after_push` now wraps) and `AppHandle::request_restart` (safe from any thread; its `ExitRequested` carries `RESTART_EXIT_CODE`, which `exit_action` always allows). The command now returns at once; `SettingsDialog` re-enables "Restart now" when it returns. The dialog and the restart are owner checks (no WebView2 here).
**No sync push before the restart** (corrected after the final M11 review, I1; this entry first claimed one): "Restart now" is offered only while a data folder change waits (`restart_required`), and `sync_runner::ready` is false then (M9 stops sync on a pending folder change), so `finish_after_push` skips the push and restarts at once. That is kept on purpose: a push from the old folder, which is about to be swapped, is not wanted. Nothing is lost: an edit made before the folder choice was copied into the new folder (when it had no data files of its own) and the first run after the restart pushes it; when the new folder already held data, the old folder's unpushed edits stay there by the user's choice.
**Tests:** `sessions::tests::a_restart_asks_like_a_close_while_a_session_is_connected`, `the_restart_dialog_says_restart_and_the_close_dialog_says_close`; `SettingsDialog.test.ts` `restart_now_calls_restart_app` (usable again after the call returns).
**Rejected alternatives:** a confirmation in the UI only (a script calling the command would still skip it); a push from the old folder before the restart (it would bypass the paused sync of a pending folder change).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 native paste: one OS clipboard read in flight

**Context:** M11 review #67 (W7): the 2 s limit of `read_with_limit` bounded each paste's wait, not the number of reader threads; against a hung clipboard owner every paste parked one more `spawn_blocking` thread until tokio's blocking pool (shared with sync, import, export and the native dialogs) was full.
**Decision:** `read_with_limit` takes an in-flight flag (`READ_IN_FLIGHT` in `clipboard.rs` for the command); while it is set a paste answers `clipboard_unavailable` at once, and a guard moved into the blocking closure clears it when the OS read returns (or when the closure is dropped unrun). At most one thread is ever stuck. Known limit: a clipboard read that never returns keeps the flag set, so menu Paste answers `clipboard_unavailable` for the rest of the run (keyboard paste does not go through `clipboard_read_text` and still works).
**Tests:** `clipboard::tests::only_one_os_read_is_in_flight_while_one_hangs` (eight pastes against a read that blocks until released: one OS read; after the release the next paste reads again; before, eight reads started).

## 2026-11-25 – M11 fix wave G+H: auto-lock, fingerprint, lock path, import text, inspect race

**Context:** M11 review #33, #34, #36, #46, #47 (all Low).
**Decision:**
- #33: `note_activity` goes through `idle_lock::note_activity_at`, which runs the lock check (`run_tick`) with the heartbeat's own clock readings before it restarts the idle clock, and the command wrapper emits `vault-status` when that locked. `IdleTracker` stays a pure clock (`note` still resets); an overdue lock wins over activity because the check comes first. The command signature gained an `AppHandle` in the wrapper only (the plain function and its `table()` entry are unchanged).
- #34: `persist::load` returns `Loaded.fingerprint`, the SHA-256 of the raw bytes (BOM included) of the main file it parsed (`None` when the tree came from `.bak` or the file was missing); `Store` uses it (a `.bak` restore still takes the fingerprint of the file it writes back). No second read.
- #36: `vault_lock` first calls `import_commands::clear_secret_pending` (pending lock, then the vault lock: the documented order) which drops an mRemoteNG plan with entries and a backup with entries; a connections plan or a picked file stays. A UI that applies after a lock gets `no_pending_import` and re-inspects. `AppState.auto_unlock_known` caches "the key store holds this vault's key" per vault id (set by remember, set-auto-unlock, create and the startup auto-unlock; one `get` only for a vault not known yet or after an unavailable store), so `view` no longer calls `KeyStore::get`. An item removed outside the app shows after a restart. Known limit: a plan with entries that was inspected while the vault was already locked stays in pending (secrets in memory) until apply, cancel or a page load; the lock clearing only runs at the moment of locking.
- #46: `limits::clean` drops the bidi marks, embeddings, overrides, isolates, U+061C, U+2028 and U+2029 (not the joiners U+200C and U+200D); the mRemoteNG reader cleans `Name`, `Descr`, `Hostname`, `Domain` and `PuttySession` at read time (hosts are cleaned silently, like the connections and backup paths; no new report line); `sanitize_nodes` cleans `settings.domain` and drops one that is empty or still invalid; backup entry names lose the same characters (entry user names and domains of a backup are login data and stay as they are).
- #47: `install` (the end of an inspect) swaps `page_reloaded` and, when it was set, clears the slot and answers `no_pending_import`.
**Tests:** `idle_lock::tests::activity_after_an_overdue_sleep_locks_instead_of_resetting`; `store::tests::open_takes_the_fingerprint_from_the_bytes_it_parsed`, `reload_takes_the_fingerprint_from_the_bytes_it_parsed`; `import_commands::tests::vault_lock_clears_a_decrypted_backup_from_pending` (and the mRemoteNG and auto-lock variants), `vault_commands::tests::lock_and_status_do_not_read_the_data_key_from_the_keychain`; `limits::tests`, `read.rs` `control_and_direction_characters_are_removed_...`, `connections.rs` and `backup.rs` direction-character cases; `import_commands::tests::an_inspect_that_finishes_after_a_skipped_page_load_drops_its_result`.

## 2026-11-25 – M11 fix wave I: dependency intake, workflow policy, release artifacts, repo hygiene

**Context:** M11 review #32 (Medium) and #69, #70, #72, #73, #74, #76, #77 (Low), all from the release review (C1 to C3, C5 to C7, C9, C10).
**Decision:**
- #32: every `updates` entry in `.github/dependabot.yml` has `cooldown: default-days: 7` (Dependabot's implicit default is 3 days). Security updates are exempt by Dependabot's design. Required reviews on `dev` were left off (owner, 2026-11-25): GitHub does not let a pull request's author approve it, and Claude opens pull requests under the owner's account, so one required approval would block every merge or be bypassed as admin each time. Instead, Claude never merges a Dependabot pull request; the owner reviews and merges those (`CLAUDE.md` "Git & branches").
- #69: `tools/check-workflows.sh` stays a dependency-free awk check (no YAML parser on the runner is assumed) but fails closed: every line more indented than a `run:` or `script:` key is checked as its value (block scalars with a comment after the indicator, comment-looking lines, continuation lines of plain and quoted scalars); a `run`/`uses`/`script` key it cannot read (flow style, quoted, spaced), a quoted value that does not close on its line, and YAML anchors, aliases and merge keys outside a run value are refused; `workflow_run` is refused like `pull_request_target`. Workflows are therefore written in plain block style. "Require actions to be pinned to a full-length commit SHA" is on (owner, 2026-11-25) and CodeQL analyses the `actions` language (`codeql.yml`), see "M11: immutable releases, draft then publish".
- #70: both checkouts in `release.yml` use `refs/tags/<tag>` from the workflow-level `RELEASE_TAG` (empty on a pull request, which keeps checking out the merge commit). The `v*` tag ruleset is on (owner, 2026-11-25).
- #72: `publish` downloads `installers-*` without `merge-multiple`, so each artifact lands in its own folder, and `verify-installers.sh --artifacts` accepts exactly the four platform folders with exactly their own files before it copies them into `installers/`. This needs no `actions: read` permission (an extra artifact shows up as an extra folder). Residual limit: a build job that replaces another job's artifact under the same name (upload with `overwrite`) is not detected; that needs per-job digests and stays with the provenance idea of C4.
- #73: the upload step is skipped for a pull request whose head repository is not this one; a fork's dry run still builds. Recorded check: the artifact list of a fork PR run is empty (needs a real fork PR).
- #74: the UI allowlist in `.gitleaks.toml` has `condition = "AND"` and `regexTarget = "line"`, so only the `STORAGE_KEY`/`KEY` lines are exempt; all allowlist paths are anchored `(^|/)` so a scan with an absolute source (`dir /repo`, `detect --no-git --source /repo`) is judged like a git scan (before, it reported 29 findings).
- #76: `.gitignore` covers `*.fjbk`, `fernjo-connections-*.json`, `*.bak`, `settings.json.bak`, `.device/`, `sync.json`, `sync-base.json`, `open-tabs.json`, `vault-marks.json`, `known_hosts`, `known_hosts.confirmed`, `rdp_certs.confirmed`, `appmenu-never` and `*.env` (hub `head.env` and `history/<seq>.env`); no tracked file is ignored by them.
- #77: `deny.toml` sets `unmaintained = "all"` and `yanked = "deny"`; the six ignores that cargo-deny 0.22 reported as not encountered are matched again and no new ignore was needed.
**Checks:** `tools/test-release-scripts.sh` (`check_workflows_rejects_form_*`, `check_workflows_accepts_the_ordinary_block_forms`, `verify_installers_artifacts_*`, `release_checkouts_use_full_tag_refs`, `release_upload_skips_fork_pull_requests`, `release_publish_keeps_each_artifact_in_its_own_folder`); gitleaks v8.30.1 probe in a scratch repository; `git check-ignore -v`; `cargo deny check advisories` without `advisory-not-detected`.

## 2026-11-25 – M11 fix wave J: webview autofill, CSP directives, DevTools-port warning, plan Debug

**Context:** M11 review #63, #64, #66, #79 (all Low; W3, W4, W6 of the webview review and a finding of the guard test).
**Decision:**
- #63 (unconfirmed, fixed as a precaution): `generalAutofillEnabled: false` on the main window (`tauri-utils` 2.10 exposes it; it maps to WebView2's `IsGeneralAutofillEnabled`; it does not touch password or credit card autofill, which WebView2 leaves to its own settings, password autosave being off by default). Every secret input opts out in its own right: `PasswordField` uses `autocomplete="new-password"` (the value Chromium honours for a password-typed field, where `off` is ignored by the password manager; the field turns into `type="text"` while shown, and `new-password` holds for both), the two reveal inputs in `VaultManager.svelte` and the pairing-code textarea use `off` (plain text, not password-typed). `ui/src/lib/secret-inputs.test.ts` scans all components and fails on a secret-looking `<input>` or `<textarea>` without one of the two values. Whether Chromium ever saved a revealed value stays unconfirmed (reading a real profile was out of bounds); the owner check is a release binary, a scratch `FERNJO_DATA_DIR`, reveal, Save, close the window and search `EBWebView\Default\Web Data` for the sentinel.
- #64: the CSP gains `form-action 'none'; base-uri 'self'; object-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'` (none falls back to `default-src`). Every `<form>` in the UI (12) handles `onsubmit` with `preventDefault`, so `form-action 'none'` breaks nothing. `base-uri 'self'` rather than `'none'` (controller ruling; the UI sets no `<base>`). `frame-ancestors` has no effect in a top-level webview and is added only as a harmless layer. `security.freezePrototype` stays off: enabling it needs a check in a real app, and WebView2 is missing on the development VM; it is an owner part (`pnpm tauri build --no-bundle`, load the app, no console errors, tree, session and vault work).
- #66: documentation only (`CLAUDE.md`, DevTools recipe): the port is unauthenticated, other local accounts can reach it and get the full command set; use it on a single-user machine, on a scratch `FERNJO_DATA_DIR`, and stop the app afterwards.
- #79: the guard's check `format!("{:?}", w.st).contains(RDP_USER)` was vacuous (`AppState`'s `Debug` prints `data_dir`, `source` and `settings_unusable`). It is removed and replaced by `rdp_plan_debug_hides_password_user_and_domain`, which resolves a real RDP plan and checks its `Debug`. That test failed at first: `RdpPlan` derived `Debug` and printed the user name and domain in clear (only the password was protected). `RdpPlan` now has a manual `Debug` that redacts both, like `ConnectParams`; a test in `sessions/rdp/tests.rs` covers the params built from the plan.
**Tests:** `src-tauri/tests/tauri_conf.rs` `csp_closes_the_directives_default_src_does_not_cover`, `main_window_turns_general_autofill_off`; `ui/src/lib/secret-inputs.test.ts`; `vault_commands::tests::rdp_plan_debug_hides_password_user_and_domain`; `sessions::rdp::tests::debug_output_of_plan_and_params_hides_user_domain_and_password`.

## 2026-11-25 – M11 RDP: NLA required, TLS-only servers refused

**Context:** findings table row 27 (`Docs/release-checklist.md`, "v0.3.0 security review"). `build_config` offered TLS alone next to CredSSP (`enable_tls: true`), and on the TLS-only path the Client Info PDU carries the user name, domain and password in the clear inside TLS. A machine in the path could answer the X.224 request with `PROTOCOL_SSL` and present its own certificate; on a first connection, or after "Replace" (Windows regenerates its self-signed certificate, which trains people to accept), the vault password reached it. With CredSSP the same machine gets at most a NetNTLMv2 response.
**Decision (owner, 2026-11-25):** NLA is required, always. `build_config` sets `enable_tls: false`, `enable_credssp: true`, so the X.224 request offers `HYBRID | HYBRID_EX` only. `connect.rs` runs the X.224 negotiation itself (`negotiate`, `ironrdp_tokio::connect_begin` with one addition) and checks the server's confirm in `check_confirm` before the connector sees it: a confirm whose selection holds neither `HYBRID` nor `HYBRID_EX` (`SSL`, standard RDP security, `RDSTLS`, `RDSAAD`, unknown bits), and a negotiation failure `SSL_REQUIRED_BY_SERVER`, `SSL_NOT_ALLOWED_BY_SERVER` or `SSL_WITH_USER_AUTH_REQUIRED_BY_SERVER`, end the attempt with the new `RdpError::NlaRequired` (code `nla_required`, text "the server does not offer Network Level Authentication (NLA); fernjo only connects to RDP servers with NLA, so the password is only ever sent inside NLA (CredSSP)"). That is before the TLS handshake, so before the certificate decision and the user name and password prompts, and the client sends nothing after the confirm. IronRDP 0.12's connector would refuse a selection outside the request too, but only with a generic `Reason` error (`protocol`), and a negotiation failure as `connect_failed`; fernjo does not rely on that check. The confirm step sends nothing, so `negotiate` writes nothing after it and ends the attempt as `protocol` should a future connector want to; other failure codes (no certificate on the server, inconsistent flags) keep that mapping.
**Tests:** `crates/fernjo-rdp/tests/connect.rs` `a_tls_only_server_is_refused_with_nla_required_before_tls_and_any_prompt`, `a_server_selecting_standard_rdp_security_is_refused_with_nla_required`, `any_other_selection_without_credssp_is_refused_with_nla_required` (`RDSTLS`, and `SSL` with an unknown bit), `a_negotiation_failure_for_lack_of_nla_is_nla_required` (no prompt, no pin, one connection only, and the fake server reads no byte after its confirm), `another_negotiation_failure_stays_connect_failed`, `the_request_offers_credssp_and_not_tls_alone`; unit tests of `check_confirm` in `connect.rs`; `sessions::rdp::tests::a_server_without_nla_has_its_own_failure_text`. The fake server (`tests/support`) now selects `HYBRID` by default, so the existing tests still reach TLS, the certificate decision and the prompts; it stops when CredSSP starts. `Behaviour::Selects` and `Behaviour::RefusesWith` are the servers without NLA.
**Cost:** a host that does not offer CredSSP no longer connects: a Windows host whose security layer is set to "RDP", some xrdp setups, very old Windows. A Windows host where NLA is merely not required (the "Allow connections only from computers running Remote Desktop with Network Level Authentication" box unticked) still offers CredSSP and connects. A per-connection opt-in ("allow servers without NLA") is a backlog item, not part of M11.
**Limits:** a real NLA host connect after this change is an owner check (`FERNJO_TEST_RDP_ADDR=host:3389 FERNJO_TEST_RDP_USER=... FERNJO_TEST_RDP_PASSWORD=... cargo test -p fernjo-rdp --test connect`). A man in the middle whose certificate is accepted still gets the NetNTLMv2 response (offline cracking of a weak password, or relay to another NTLM service in the network, which CredSSP's public key binding does not stop); the real server, once trusted, gets the password through CredSSP's credential delegation by protocol.

## 2026-11-25 – M11: owner decisions on the security review (2026-11-25)

**Context:** the M11 security review (`Docs/release-checklist.md`, "v0.3.0 security review", rows 21 to 80) left decisions that only the owner can take: what to fix against a recommendation, which remaining risks to accept, which repo settings to change. The owner answered the batch with "all as recommended" on 2026-11-25. This entry is the record; the `decision` cell of each row says the same in one line. No High is open. The Medium findings are all fixed (rows 22 to 32 and 80) except two parts: the rest of row 22 is accepted (below) and the repo setting of row 32 is the owner's.
**Decided, fixed:** row 27 (Medium): NLA is required, a TLS-only or standard-security RDP server is refused with `nla_required` (entry "M11 RDP: NLA required, TLS-only servers refused"; a per-connection opt-in stays a backlog item). Row 71: build provenance is attested in the `publish` job and checkable with `gh attestation verify` (README); it has not run yet, the first run is the v0.3.0 release.
**Accepted Medium (remaining risk):** row 22, a frozen SSH tab is not detected. A server that stops reading no longer leaks the socket or tasks (bounded close, writes race the session end), but nothing notices that the terminal froze while the tab stays "connected"; the user closes it. Not fixed because the only detector tried, russh's `inactivity_timeout`, cut healthy sessions after a Windows sleep (entry "M11 SSH: a server that stops reading"). Backlog: "Detect a frozen SSH tab".
**Accepted Lows, with the remaining risk and why they stay:**
- Row 35, `settings.json` sits in the default data folder: whoever syncs that folder lets a writer of it change this device's security settings (auto-lock off, `dataDir`); moving the file is not small. The note for an unusable file is a backlog item.
- Row 36 (known limit of the fix): a plan inspected while the vault is already locked stays pending until the page loads again, it is applied or cancelled; it holds no secret that the lock should have dropped.
- Row 37, a locked device writes an older-generation head over its vault: the newer local file is one save away from gone until the next unlock (then `rolled_back`). Only a trusted peer can produce such a head, and a locked vault cannot read the body to compare.
- Row 38, no ACL hardening of the data folder on Windows: an account that can already read the user profile (administrator, backup software) reads the tree and the encrypted vault. Unix has 0600 and 0700.
- Row 40, a hung network share blocks one blocking thread inside `KnownHosts` I/O (a FIFO is refused and the I/O left the async workers).
- Row 41, russh buffers outgoing data without bound while the peer reads nothing: a webview script (assumption 4) can grow memory, and nothing bounds the time except closing the tab (the inactivity timeout that row 41 first leaned on was dropped with row 22).
- Row 42, no binding of a credential to a host: a data-folder writer can swap the hosts of two connections the device trusts, so credential A reaches host B's operator without a prompt (needs folder write plus two trusted hosts).
- Row 45, the RDP password sits in IronRDP's plain `String` config: it may stay in freed memory after the session (the same class as the accepted secret residue).
- Row 51, snapshot field lengths are bounded only by the 64 MiB envelope: a trusted peer (assumption 2) can bloat the tree up to that cap.
- Row 55, `sync_pairing_code` works without an unlocked vault: anyone at the unlocked OS session, or a webview script, reads the hub secret and sync key without the master password; requiring an unlock would break pairing for groups without a vault.
- Row 56, the sync key is never rotated: a removed or lost device keeps the key and can decrypt heads it can still fetch (rotating the hub secret cuts its hub access). Backlog: "Sync key rotation".
- Row 57, the hub has no request timeout or connection limit of its own: a hub exposed without the reverse proxy of `Docs/hub.md` can be held open by slow clients.
- Row 62, the capability grants more than the UI uses: accepted for now, a script can forge status events and create tray or menu items. `core:default` is narrowed to `core:event:allow-listen` and `allow-unlisten` after a check in a real build (this development VM lacks the WebView2 runtime). Backlog: "Narrow the webview capability".
- Row 68, the no-secret guard reaches only `session_not_connected` for `rdp_input` and `rdp_clipboard`: a secret in a connected-path error of those two would not be caught (both return fixed errors today).
- Row 75, `tools/ssh-test-server.sh` keeps state in a fixed `/tmp` folder: developer-only, not shipped; a hostile local user on a shared Linux box can kill one developer process and block the test server.
**Acknowledged design changes** (behaviour that changed on purpose and that the owner has seen): row 24, a host key rotated on one device raises the changed-key prompt on the other devices, because the device's confirmed file now outranks the synced `known_hosts`, and there is no forget-host UI yet (backlog "Forget a host key"); row 21, a data file that is a symlink is refused (a symlinked folder above it still works); row 53, release builds refuse a plain `http://` hub, also on loopback; row 28, a repeated key inside one JSON object makes a data file unreadable (the last-wins behaviour of the old `Value` step is gone).
**Repo settings:** settled on 2026-11-25 (see "M11: immutable releases, draft then publish" and the #32 line of "M11 fix wave I: dependency intake, workflow policy, release artifacts, repo hygiene"): SHA pinning and the `v*` tag ruleset are on; CodeQL for `actions` is in `codeql.yml`; required reviews on `dev` were replaced by the rule that only the owner merges Dependabot pull requests; immutable releases are on since 2026-11-25, after the draft-then-publish `release.yml` reached `dev`.
**Not put to the owner:** row 52, a native confirmation that names the hub host before `sync_setup` and `sync_join`; the batch did not contain it, so it is a backlog item (a webview script, adversary 7, can still start a sync to a hub of its own; the row is documented in the threat model, cell 7). It costs one more milestone item if the owner wants it before the release.
**Backlog entries written** (`Docs/roadmap.json`): "Forget a host key", "Narrow the webview capability", "Native confirmation for sync setup and join", "Note when auto-lock is off because settings.json is unusable", "Detect a frozen SSH tab", "Sync key rotation", "RDP: allow servers without NLA per connection" and "M11 review leftovers" (the deferred minors of the M11 task reviews that are still open).

## 2026-11-25 – M12: hub image on GHCR

**Context:** the hub was only buildable from source (`Docs/hub.md`), so running it meant building it. M12 publishes it as a ready-made image with each release. This entry records how, and why.
**Decided:**
- **Registry and tags:** `ghcr.io/benjo-code/fernjo-hub`, in the same organisation as the repository. The workflow's own `GITHUB_TOKEN` can push there, and the build provenance is attested next to the image; no extra account or secret. Tags `X.Y.Z` (the one to pin), `X.Y` and `latest`, only from a pushed `v*` tag; `tools/hub-image-tags.sh` computes them and refuses anything that is not plain `MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH`, so a stray tag like `v0.3.1-rc.1` never moves `latest`.
- **Two native runners, no emulation:** `linux/amd64` on `ubuntu-latest` and `linux/arm64` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, joined into one manifest list. Emulating arm64 under QEMU would run the Rust compile many times slower.
- **Build and test first, push the same bits:** job `hub-image-build` builds each architecture, smoke-tests it (`tools/smoke-hub-image.sh`: `init` on a fresh volume as user 10001, `serve`, `/v1/info` with and without the secret, a stop that returns in under 5 s with exit status 0) and saves an OCI archive; job `hub-image` downloads both archives and pushes exactly those as the manifest list, checks that the three tags share one digest, checks that both architectures are inside and attests the digest. A failing smoke test therefore blocks the push, and the tested image is the pushed image.
- **`packages: write` only in the push job:** `hub-image` is the one job with `packages: write`, `id-token: write` and `attestations: write`; the build jobs, which also run on pull requests, keep `contents: read`. Podman is used on the runners (preinstalled there) because it is the owner's own tool.
- **Pull requests are the dry run:** a pull request that touches the Containerfile, the deploy files, `.dockerignore` or `release.yml` builds and smoke-tests both architectures and pushes nothing; the archive is not uploaded from a fork. Paths filter the whole workflow, so such a pull request also runs the installer builds.
- **No image for v0.3.0:** that release's tag and workflow are fixed (immutable tag ruleset). The first image is v0.3.1; the deploy files pin `0.3.1` before the release bump, and `tools/check-release-version.sh` now also fails a release whose Compose file or Quadlet unit pins another tag than the release version, so the pins cannot go stale.
- **Compose is the main path, Quadlet stays supported:** `compose.yaml` works with Docker and Podman; the rootless mapping `userns_mode: keep-id:uid=10001,gid=10001` is only in `compose.podman.yaml` because Docker rejects it. The Quadlet unit uses the same pinned image.
- **Image labels:** the Containerfile takes `VERSION` and `REVISION` build arguments and sets the OCI labels `source`, `version`, `revision`, `licenses` and `description`; the `source` label also links the package to this repository on the first push.
- **Review fixes (final whole-branch review):** `publish` needs `hub-image`, because a published release is immutable and its deploy files pin the image (before, a failed push would have published a release with dead pins; a failed run now leaves a draft to repeat). The digest check also asks the registry what each tag holds, not only what Podman reported. The attestation is verified with `--signer-workflow` and `--source-ref refs/tags/vX.Y.Z`, like the installers. Accepted: a rebuild by `workflow_dispatch` after the artifacts expired can produce another digest and move `X.Y.Z` while the release is still a draft (the attestation then names the new digest); this only happens before anything is published.
**Rejected:**
- QEMU emulation for arm64 (slow compile, one more moving part).
- Each build job pushing its own architecture, then a job joining them: the build jobs would need `packages: write`, and what is pushed would no longer be what was smoke-tested.
- A new action (for example a Docker login or buildx action): the plan stays with the pinned actions already used in `release.yml` and the tools preinstalled on the runners.
**Open until the v0.3.1 tag:** the push itself (a registry login, `podman manifest push` of OCI archives, the attestation with `push-to-registry`) can only run on a tag push, so its first run is the release run; the package is private after that first push and the owner makes it public once (`Docs/hub.md`).
